<a id="q5"></a>

## Q5. For height-h Morava E-theory, let Γ be the additive-power-operation algebra and …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.4.1.1) · **Collection** 1

For height-h Morava E-theory, let Γ be the additive-power-operation algebra and σ its suspension endomorphism. For n>0, is Tor_(j)^(Γ)(E_(0),Γ)≠0 whenever 0≤j&lt;h? The left action on the second factor is twisted by σ^(n); E_(0) has the trivial right action.

**Context.** Origin: Both conjectures arise from dissertation research with Andrew Senger, following the proved special cases.

**Source.** Eunice Sukarto. *Power operations modulo Lubin-Tate parameters*. Harvard University, 2026. Advisor(s): Michael Hopkins; Andrew Senger. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/316880b6-37d3-4812-a41f-220ca9955de1/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/handle/1/42740462) Location: Conjecture 3.4.1.1, printed p. 133 (PDF p. 144); §3.4.1. Search-qualified status: The accessible Senger–Sukarto manuscript still states it as Conjecture 9.0.1, with only the height-two example. No later general resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~esukarto/power_operations_2.pdf) · [2](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~esukarto/)


<a id="q6"></a>

## Q6. For every prime p, is e_(k)A[k]→e_(k−1)B^(′)[k−1] injective with image η\_(k−1)e_…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.2.4.14) · **Collection** 1

For every prime p, is e_(k)A[k]→e_(k−1)B^(′)[k−1] injective with image η\_(k−1)e_(k−1)B^(′)[k−1]? Here S[k] is the power-operation ring (3.11), A[k]=S[k]/(η\_(k)), B^(′)[k−1]=S[k]/(t_(1)), e_(k) is the GL_(k)(𝔽\_(p)) Steinberg idempotent, and η\_(k) is the product of nonzero 𝔽\_(p)-linear formal-group combinations of coordinates. The map is §3.2.4.1’s projection/induction map; p=2 is proved.

**Context.** Origin: Both conjectures arise from dissertation research with Andrew Senger, following the proved special cases.

**Source.** Eunice Sukarto. *Power operations modulo Lubin-Tate parameters*. Harvard University, 2026. Advisor(s): Michael Hopkins; Andrew Senger. ## Q120. Let G be connected reductive over C and X a smooth affine spherical G-variety eq…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.2.15) · **Collection** 2

Let G be connected reductive over C and X a smooth affine spherical G-variety equal to the affine closure of its open orbit, with no normalized spherical roots of type N. For I an Iwahori subgroup and M̌ the BZSV dual Hamiltonian Ǧ-space, does Shv_I^(c,Sat)(X((t));Q)≃QCoh(sh^(1/2)(M̌×\_(ǧ\*)g̃̌)/Ǧ), sending IC₀=i_!(Q) to the structure sheaf and intertwining the affine-Hecke action under Bezrukavnikov’s equivalence? Here i:X[[t]]→X((t)) includes positive loops; the source is generated from IC₀ by Hecke convolution; g̃̌=T\*(Ǧ/Ň)/Ť is the Grothendieck–Springer resolution; half-shearing shifts weight w by cohomological degree w. Type N is the rank-one PGL₂/normalizer-of-a-torus case.

**Context.** Origin: Own Iwahori-level extension, explicitly attributed to Devalapurkar by Lin–Pham–Yu. It first appeared in his 2024 paper as Conjecture 3.4.14, before dissertation filing; related earlier/concurrent formulations by Finkelberg–Ginzburg–Travkin are disclosed in the thesis. Advisors verified on dissertation abstract page and author’s current homepage.

**Source.** Sanath Devalapurkar. *Spherochromatism in representation theory and arithmetic geometry*. Harvard University, 2025. Advisor(s): Michael Hopkins; Dennis Gaitsgory. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/c2637f18-5416-4b41-8eed-b9074cf2c1f0/content) · [record](https://dash.harvard.edu/entities/publication/86c5bb12-70a7-4d5e-9f35-c3c6a4385c18) Location: Conjecture 5.2.15, deposited thesis printed p. 249 / PDF p. 262; type-N convention Remarks 5.1.23–24. Author’s January 2026 revision: same conjecture, printed p. 172 / PDF p. 181. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: Substantial partial progress: Lin–Pham–Yu, arXiv:2510.25231, text dated 2026-08-24, proves a D-module variant assuming unramified relative duality, placidity and dimension theory. Its introduction explicitly does not establish compatibility with Bezrukavnikov’s action, a required part here. arXiv:2601.18022 establishes quasi-split symmetric-space cases. No unrestricted theorem resolving the stated Betti-sheaf conjecture and full compatibility was located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2510.25231) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.18022) · [3](https://sanathdevalapurkar.github.io/files/thesis.pdf) · [4](https://sanathdevalapurkar.github.io/)

## Q121. For every almost classical (integrally null-homologous) flat knot α, does J̄α(−1…

**Context.** Origin: The author proposes these problems about his new flat-knot invariants, computations, and slice constructions. Shared notation: A flat knot is an immersed oriented circle in an oriented closed surface, modulo homotopy and stabilization. For its minimal-genus n-crossing diagram, J^(en)\_α(w,z)=(−1)ⁿ∑\_S(−2)^(k(S)) z^(r(S)) w^(m(S)), over smoothings S. Here k,r are kernel/image dimensions of H₁(S;F₂)→H₁(Σ;F₂), and m counts homologically nonzero components. Put Jα(z)=J^(en)\_α(1,z).

**Source.** Jie Chen. *Flat knots and invariants*. McMaster University, 2023. Advisor(s): Hans U. Boden. [primary source](https://math.mcmaster.ca/~boden/students/Chen-PhD.pdf) · [record](https://math.mcmaster.ca/graduate/graduate-degrees-awarded/degrees-awarded-phd-math/) Location: Conjecture 5.11, printed p. 67 / PDF p. 76; normalization Definition 5.9, p. 66. Given a finite presentation of a one-ended hyperbolic group G and generators of a quasiconvex subgroup H, can an algorithm decide whether G has a nontrivial amalgamated-product or HNN splitting over a subgroup commensurable with H, without assuming H residually finite? Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The final paper, online 2024 and in the 2026 journal volume, still assumes residual finiteness or additional information about H. No later unconditional decision algorithm or undecidability theorem was found in exact-topic searches and the author’s current publications.

## Q189. Let F : X^(m) → Y^(n) be a smooth map between closed orientable Riemannian manif…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 2

Let F : X^(m) → Y^(n) be a smooth map between closed orientable Riemannian manifolds, m > n and k > (m + 1)/2. Is the existence, for every ε > 0, of F_(ε) ≃ F with dil_(k)(F_(ε)) < ε equivalent to F_(\*)H_(j)(X; ℤ) = 0 for all j ≥ k and F_(\*)(a) = 0 for every torsion a ∈ H_(k − 1)(X; ℤ)? Here dil_(k)(F) = sup_(x)∥⋀^(k)dF_(x)∥.

**Context.** Origin: Immediately before Conjecture 1 the author describes developing evidence for this conjecture during his PhD. The torsion obstruction is identified as his recent result; the thesis proves special cases k=n and n−1, leaving sufficiency in general open. The chapter starts with a broader question from advisor Larry Guth. After proving its k>n+1 regime, Kumanduri isolates the remaining even-n critical case as Question 3. The record attributes this refinement to the thesis and discloses its advisor-supplied motivation.

**Source.** Luis Kumanduri. *Homotopically nontrivial area contracting maps*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisor(s): Larry Guth. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/e459cb87-f940-4b01-a40c-74c77cf930c6/content) · [record](https://math.mit.edu/research/graduate/thesis-defenses.html?year=2023) Location: Conjecture 1, printed p. 14; definition of k-dilation §1.1. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The thesis proves only stated special cases. Searches through 2026-10-05 found no general resolution. Portnoy 2025 cites and uses this work for parametrized isoperimetry, without a general homological characterization.

**Further links.** [1](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/dd236b18-0619-4aef-aaab-f0d8f0680a59) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.01456)

## Q190. For every positive even integer n and smooth map f : S^(2n + 1) → ℂℙ^(n), does e…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 2

For every positive even integer n and smooth map f : S^(2n + 1) → ℂℙ^(n), does every ε > 0 admit a homotopic smooth f_(ε) with dil_(n + 1)(f_(ε)) < ε? The metrics are fixed; dil_(k)(f) = sup_(x)∥⋀^(k)df_(x)∥.

**Context.** Origin: Immediately before Conjecture 1 the author describes developing evidence for this conjecture during his PhD. The torsion obstruction is identified as his recent result; the thesis proves special cases k=n and n−1, leaving sufficiency in general open. The chapter starts with a broader question from advisor Larry Guth. After proving its k>n+1 regime, Kumanduri isolates the remaining even-n critical case as Question 3. The record attributes this refinement to the thesis and discloses its advisor-supplied motivation.

**Source.** Luis Kumanduri. *Homotopically nontrivial area contracting maps*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisor(s): Larry Guth. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/e459cb87-f940-4b01-a40c-74c77cf930c6/content) · [record](https://math.mit.edu/research/graduate/thesis-defenses.html?year=2023) Location: Question 3, printed p. 28. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The thesis resolves k>n+1, not critical k=n+1. Checked Tsai–Tsui–Wang 2025, latest arXiv revision 28 September 2025: Corollary 1.6 concerns a 2-dilation obstruction and does not settle (n+1)-dilation for even n. Topic/author searches found no later resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.09222) · [2](https://d-nb.info/1383092168/34)

## Q201. For \$n\ge1,m\ge0\$, use the degree-\$m\$ Fock component \$F^m_{A_{2n-1}}\$ over \$\mat…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.5.1) · **Collection** 3

For \$n\ge1,m\ge0\$, use the degree-\$m\$ Fock component \$F^m_{A_{2n-1}}\$ over \$\mathbb Z[t,t^{-1}]\$ and Schur-labelled creation/annihilation operators \$p_i^\lambda,q_i^\lambda\$ (§§3.3–3.4). For a partition \$\lambda\$ with Durfee-square side \$r\$, right/bottom remainders \$\alpha,\beta\$, put \$d_\lambda=|\alpha|-|\beta|+r\$ and \$\epsilon_\lambda=0\$ if \$\lambda=\lambda^T\$, otherwise \$2\$. For \$1\le i<2n\$, define \$\Sigma_{i,m}=\sum_{|\lambda|\le m}(-1)^{d_\lambda}t^{-d_\lambda}p_i^\lambda q_i^{\lambda^T}\$ and \$\Sigma^{\prime}\_{i,m}=\sum_{|\lambda|\le m}(-1)^{d_\lambda+\epsilon_\lambda}t^{d_\lambda+\epsilon_\lambda}p_i^\lambda q_i^{\lambda^T}\$. Are these mutually inverse generators of a \$B_{2n}\$-action on every \$F^m_{A_{2n-1}}\$?

**Context.** Origin: New braid-action conjecture for operators constructed in §3.5. The 2023 arXiv version is a revised dissertation; the doctorate is dated 2022.

**Source.** Kie Seng Nge. *From Homological Algebra to Topology via Type B Zigzag Algebra and Heisenberg Algebra*. Australian National University, Australia, 2022. Advisor(s): Anthony M. Licata; Uri Onn (associate supervisor); Asilata Bapat (associate supervisor). Operator definitions on printed p. 84, PDF p. 102. Title-page supervision verified.

## Q209. Does some \$W\$ built using only subcritical handles contain a closed exact \$L\$ wi…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 3

Does some \$W\$ built using only subcritical handles contain a closed exact \$L\$ with nonzero Maslov class when attaching spheres need not lie over a contractible base neighborhood?

**Context.** Origin: New questions relaxing assumptions in Husin–Kragh’s work and Husin’s handle constructions. Shared setup for questions 209, 210, 211: Let \$X\$ be a closed \$n\$-manifold and \$W\$ a Weinstein domain formed from \$D^\*X\$ by handle attachments. Require a quadratic complex volume form \$\Omega^2\$, trivializing \$(\det_{\mathbb C}T^\*W)^{\otimes2}\$ and extending the standard one. A closed Lagrangian \$L\$ is exact when \$\lambda|\_L=df\$; its Maslov class measures winding of the \$\Omega^2\$-phase. Handles of index \$&lt;n\$ are subcritical, and index \$n\$ is critical.

**Source.** Axel Husin. *Generalized local systems and the Maslov class of exact Lagrangians*. Uppsala University, 2025. Advisor(s): Thomas Kragh (primary); Tobias Ekholm (co-supervisor). [primary source](https://www.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2%3A1939913/FULLTEXT01.pdf) · [record](https://www.uu.se/en/department/mathematics/events/archive/2025-04-14-dissertation-generalized-local-systems-and-the-maslov-class-of-exact-lagrangians) Location: Chapter 7, question 1, printed p. 27 / PDF p. 27.

## Q385. Let \$\operatorname{coh}\_{\mathrm{BP}}(p)=\max\\{n:\mathrm{BP}\_{(p)}\text{ admits …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3.1) · **Collection** 4

Let \$\operatorname{coh}\_{\mathrm{BP}}(p)=\max\\{n:\mathrm{BP}\_{(p)}\text{ admits an }E_n\text{ multiplication}\\}\$. Is this function constant across primes \$p\$? If not, determine its dependence on \$p\$.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Eunice Sukarto → Andrew Senger: https://people.math.harvard.edu/~esukarto/
Andrew Senger → Haynes Miller: https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/thesis/senger-thesis.pdf Origin: Questions raised by Senger’s obstruction results, first posed in his 2017 preprint, incorporated into the 2021 thesis, and retained in the 2024 publication. Shared setup for questions 385, 386, 387: \$\mathrm{BP}\_{(p)}\$ denotes the \$p\$-local Brown–Peterson spectrum. Its height-\$h\$ truncation \$\mathrm{BP}\langle h\rangle\$ has homotopy ring \$\mathbb Z_{(p)}[v_1,\ldots,v_h]\$, with \$|v_i|=2(p^i-1)\$. An \$E_n\$ multiplication is a little-\$n\$-disks algebra structure; \$E_\infty\$ means coherently commutative multiplication. For the truncated-spectrum questions, use \$p\$-completion, as required by Remark 1.3.4 to remove ambiguity among forms.

**Source.** Andrew Senger. *Multiplicative Structures on Brown–Peterson Spectra at Odd Primes*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2021. Advisor(s): Haynes Miller. [primary source](https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/thesis/senger-thesis.pdf) · [record](https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/gradstud.html) Location: Question 1.3.1, printed/PDF p. 11.

**Further links.** [1](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~senger/cv.pdf) · [2](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~esukarto/) · [3](https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/senger24.pdf) · [4](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.14380v3) · [5](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.09822)

## Q394. Fix \$n\in\\{3,4,6\\}\$ and positive finite topological parameter \$m\$. Among compact…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Fix \$n\in\\{3,4,6\\}\$ and positive finite topological parameter \$m\$. Among compact connected topological generalized \$n\$-gons whose point rows and line pencils both have covering dimension \$m\$, must the point spaces, line spaces and flag spaces respectively have the same homotopy types? More strongly, are their homeomorphism types determined by \$n\$ and \$m\$? A flag is an incident point–line pair. The equal-parameter restriction is essential for quadrangles; unequal-parameter counterexamples are excluded.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Sira Busch → Linus Kramer: https://sirabusch.de/dissertationSiraBusch.pdf
Linus Kramer → Theo Grundhöfer: https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0104064 Origin: The author explicitly proposes a unified conjecture after his new Schubert-cell and homotopy results. Its projective-plane component is an older open problem; the dissertation’s proposal includes equal-parameter quadrangles and hexagons and the point, line and flag spaces together. This historical overlap is disclosed rather than presented as a newly invented projective-plane problem.

**Source.** Linus Kramer. *Compact Polygons*. Eberhard Karls University of Tübingen, 1994. Advisor(s): Theo Grundhöfer. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0104064) · [record](https://www.uni-muenster.de/AGKramer/index.php?lang=en&menu=members&name=lkpub) Location: Chapter 3 introductory discussion, printed p. 22 / PDF p. 29; Definition 3.1.2 defines topological parameters.

## Q395. Let \$\Gamma\$ be a height-\$n\$ formal group over a perfect field \$k\$ of characteri…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Let \$\Gamma\$ be a height-\$n\$ formal group over a perfect field \$k\$ of characteristic \$p\$, with \$n>1\$. Put \$E_n=E(k,\Gamma)\$, with \$\pi_\*E_n=W(k)[[u_1,\ldots,u_{n-1}]][u,u^{-1}]\$, and \$K_n=E_n/(p,u_1,\ldots,u_{n-1})\$. For every \$A_\infty\$ \$E_n\$-algebra structure on this two-periodic Morava K-theory, is \$\pi_\*\mathrm{THH}^{\bullet}\_{E_n}(K_n)\$ finite as a module over \$\pi_\*E_n\$? Here \$\mathrm{THH}^{\bullet}\$ denotes topological Hochschild cohomology, equivalently the derived endomorphism spectrum of \$K_n\$ as an \$E_n\$-relative \$K_n\$-bimodule. The height-one case is proved in the thesis.

**Context.** Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Eunice Sukarto → Andrew Senger: https://people.math.harvard.edu/~esukarto/
Andrew Senger → Haynes Miller: https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/thesis/senger-thesis.pdf
Vigleik Angeltveit → Haynes Miller: https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/8b2a3796-42b6-4858-98b8-7d04805eed35/content Origin: The author explicitly conjectures finiteness while discussing his new THH calculations and noncommutative multiplications. This is the two-periodic conjecture, distinguished from his separate conjecture for the (2p^n−2)-periodic theory K(n).

**Source.** Vigleik Angeltveit. *Noncommutative ring spectra*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2006. Advisor(s): Haynes Miller. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/8b2a3796-42b6-4858-98b8-7d04805eed35/content) · [record](https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/gradstud.html) Location: Unnumbered conjecture in Introduction, p. 11, restated in Chapter 6 overview, p. 47; coefficient-ring and quotient definitions, §8.2, p. 63 (PDF page numbers agree).

**Further links.** [1](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~esukarto/) · [2](https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/thesis/senger-thesis.pdf) · [3](https://msp.org/gt/2008/12-2/gt-v12-n2-p08-p.pdf) · [4](https://arxiv.org/pdf/0810.5032) · [5](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.21567v1)

## Q1118. Compact-group characters on canonical knot components

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

For every hyperbolic knot K⊂S³, does each canonical component of its SL₂(C) character variety contain a real curve of SU(2) characters? Canonical means an irreducible component containing a discrete-faithful character.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1118, 1119: The canonical-character conjecture is due to Chinburg–Reid–Stover; dominance is posed as thesis future work. Setup for questions 1118, 1119: K₁≥K₀ means a smooth annular concordance C⊂S³×[0,1], with K₀ at 0 and K₁ at 1, whose height has only index-0/1 critical points. Write Yᵢ=S³∖Kᵢ, W=(S³×[0,1])∖C, and R(V)=Hom(π₁V,SL₂(C)).

**Source.** James Patrick Dix. *Ribbon Concordances and Representation Varieties*. 2024. Advisor(s): Ian Agol. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt27j2v475/qt27j2v475.pdf) Location: Conj.3.1.7, p.10.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1118, 1119, checked 6 October 2026: The character conjecture is reaffirmed in the 2026 K3 problem list; no resolution of general dominance found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.21109v1) · [2](https://bpb-us-e2.wpmucdn.com/websites.umass.edu/dist/b/22144/files/2026/04/K3-problem-list-watermarked.pdf) · [3](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/27j2v475)

## Q1125. Finitely many ribbon predecessors

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

Does every knot K⊂S³ have only finitely many isotopy classes J with J≤K?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1125, 1126: Baldwin–Sivek conjecture restated; nonfibered extension newly posed. Setup for questions 1125, 1126: J≤K denotes smooth ribbon concordance from J to K. Write Σ\_n(K) for the n-fold cyclic branched cover, K_n for the lifted branch knot, and v₃ for the volume of a regular ideal hyperbolic tetrahedron. Floer dimensions use F₂ coefficients.

**Source.** John A. Baldwin; Jonathan Hanselman; Steven Sivek. *Ribbon concordance and fibered predecessors, II: the general case*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.21109v1) Location: Conj.1.1.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

For fixed positive integers M,g, are there only finitely many genus-g knots K⊂S³ satisfying dim ĤFK(Σ\_n(K),K_n)≤Mⁿ for infinitely many primes n, without assuming K fibered? Must every such K also satisfy ||S³∖K||≤(3π/v₃)(2g−1)log M, where ||·|| is simplicial volume?

## Q1130. Stable smoothing of isotopic four-manifold diffeomorphisms

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

If two self-diffeomorphisms of a closed smooth 4-manifold are topologically isotopic, must they become smoothly isotopic after stabilization by connected summing with finitely many S²×S² and extending the diffeomorphisms by the identity?

**Context.** Origin for question 1130: Authors’ explicit motivating question.

**Source.** Patrick Orson; Mark Powell; Oscar Randal-Williams. *Smoothing topological pseudo-isotopies of 4-manifolds*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.16984v1) Location: Q1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1130, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: source distinguishes open isotopy problem from its pseudo-isotopy counterexamples; no resolution found.

<a id="q1131"></a>

## Q1131. From optimal ribbon to complex cobordism

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

If quasipositive K₀,K₁ admit an optimal ribbon cobordism, must they admit a complex cobordism?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1131, 1132: Authors’ new questions, retained in accepted v3. Setup for questions 1131, 1132: Knots lie in S³; all cobordisms are smooth, oriented, connected. Optimal means genus |g₄(K₁)−g₄(K₀)|. Ribbon means the height function has no local maxima. Complex means cut from a nonsingular complex algebraic curve in C² between two concentric 3-spheres meeting it transversely.

**Source.** Maciej Borodzik; Paula Truöl. *Non-complex cobordisms between quasipositive knots*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.04894v3) Location: Q1.4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1131, 1132, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: no resolution found. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.matpur.2025.103842

## Q1218. Abstract Hopficity without planar ends

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.4) · **Collection** 13

For every connected noncompact orientable boundaryless surface M with no planar ends, is every subgroup H≤Map(M) containing Γ(M) and satisfying [H:H∩PMap(M)]≤ℵ₀ Hopfian as an abstract group, meaning every surjective endomorphism is injective?

**Context.** Origin for question 1218: Joint Aramayona–De Pool–Skipper–Tao–Vlamis–Wu question (2024), restated in thesis. Setup for question 1218: Map(M) denotes orientation-preserving mapping classes with the quotient compact-open topology; PMap(M) fixes every end. Γ(M) is the closure of compactly supported mapping classes.

**Source.** Rodrigo De Pool. *Rigidity results for the mapping class group*. 2025. Advisor(s): Javier Aramayona. [primary source](https://www.icmat.es/Thesis/2025/Tesis_Rodrigo_de_Pool.pdf) Location: Question 4.4, printed p. 96; definitions4.1, p. 95.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.3.10) · **Collection** 13

## Q1225. Convergence of normalized least dilatations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question Q) · **Collection** 13

Does δ\_g^g converge as g→∞, and if so, what is its limit?

**Context.** Origin for question 1225: Question 1 restates an older least-dilatation problem. Setup for question 1225: δ\_g is the least pseudo-Anosov dilatation on the closed oriented genus-g surface. For a compact fibered hyperbolic 3-manifold M and fibered Thurston-norm face F, δ\_g(M,F) minimizes monodromy dilatation among primitive integral classes in the cone over F with genus-g fibers and no one-prong boundary singularities; restrict g to nonempty such sets.

**Source.** Aidan A. Lorenz. *Small Dilatation Pseudo-Anosovs Coming from Dehn Fillings of Hyperbolic Fibered 3-Manifolds*. 2024. Advisor(s): Spencer Dowdall. [primary source](https://ir.vanderbilt.edu/bitstreams/c9e07726-4e38-4b44-9496-e034da85c5fa/download) Location: Question Q 1, p. 4.

<a id="q1324"></a>

## Q1324. Recovering Anosov orbits from Liouville geometry

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

If two AL structures are exact symplectomorphic after completion, must the underlying Anosov flows be orbit equivalent? Exact means λ₁=φ\*λ₀+df with f constant near infinity; orbit equivalence is a homeomorphism preserving oriented trajectories.

**Context.** Origin for question 1324: Massoni’s converse question, restated jointly. Setup for question 1324: An AL structure for a smooth oriented Anosov flow Φ on a closed connected oriented 3-manifold M is λ=e^(−s)α₋+e^sα₊ on R×M, where α₋,α₊ form a transverse negative/positive Liouville pair supporting Φ; thus dλ is symplectic and kerα₋∩kerα₊ is the oriented flow line field.

**Source.** Jonathan Bowden; Thomas Massoni. *Topological invariance of Liouville structures for taut foliations and Anosov flows*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2510.15325v2) Location: §0.4,Q3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1324, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: retained in July 2026 v2; skewed R-covered case proved.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B7: submitted unresolved): No new analysis or status promotion in this return. Remaining/limits: not_attempted Intake: Scope and identity checked; no independent proof or renewed literature audit of this question. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15325)

## Q1331. Finite spectral-completion fibers at minimal dimension

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

If V=γ-supp(L) is compact for some L∈L̂₀ and dim_H(V)≤n, are there only finitely many L′∈L̂₀ with γ-supp(L′)=V?

**Context.** Origin for question 1331: Conjecture from Viterbo’s CIME 2025 lectures. Setup for question 1331: For closed connected Nⁿ, let L̂₀ be the completion, in Viterbo’s spectral metric γ=c₊−c₋, of closed exact Lagrangians Hamiltonian-isotopic to the zero section in T\*N; use Z₂ coefficients. A point z belongs to γ-supp(L) iff every neighborhood U of z supports a compactly supported Hamiltonian diffeomorphism φ with φ(L)≠L in L̂₀.

**Source.** Olga Bernardi; Francesco Morabito. *The completion of the set of Lagrangians and applications to dynamics — Based on lectures by C. Viterbo*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.09396v1) Location: §4.3,(C),p. 31.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1331, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: retained in March 2026 notes; only smooth-support uniqueness known.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09396)

## Q1420. Unstabilized Legendrian generalized Property R

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.6) · **Collection** 15

Is every tight Legendrian R-link contact-(+1) handleslide equivalent to a max-tb unlink, without adjoining extra unlink components?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1420, 1421: Questions posed in this paper. Setup for questions 1420, 1421: In (S³,ξ\_st), a g-component topological R-link has an integral surgery yielding #^g(S¹×S²). It is a tight Legendrian R-link if contact-(+1) surgery yields the unique tight contact structure on that manifold. A max-tb unlink has tb=−1 on each component.

**Source.** Joseph Breen and Alexander Zupan. *Derivative links in contact topology*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.30182v1) Location: Question 1.6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1420, 1421, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: v1 dated September 24 checked; only the stabilized Legendrian statement is proved. No subsequent resolutions found.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 15

## Q1427. No positive spinning anywhere in Vol3

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 15

For every embedded closed geodesic γ in Vol3, is it impossible to positively spin an ideal triangulation along γ?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1427, 1428: Vol3 question traces to Hodgson–Weeks/Trnkova; the cusped triangulation conjecture is older, cited to Yoshida and Petronio. Setup for questions 1427, 1428: A positive spun triangulation of (N,γ) is an ideal triangulation of N∖γ with tetrahedral shapes zᵢ satisfying Im(zᵢ)>0, whose metric completion is isometric to N. Vol3 is m007(3,1) in SnapPy’s census.

**Source.** David Futer, Jessica S. Purcell and Saul Schleimer. *Hyperbolic manifolds without positive spun triangulations*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.08473v1) Location: Conjecture 1.3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1427, 1428, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: v1 checked. Only Vol3’s two shortest geodesic classes are excluded; finite-cover results do not settle the cusped conjecture.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08473)

<a id="q1429"></a>

## Q1429. Smooth classification of Zoll manifolds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.2) · **Collection** 15

Is every SC-manifold diffeomorphic to its model CROSS?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1429, 1430: Q3.2 is Besse’s older Question 7.72; C3.8 is proposed here. Setup for questions 1429, 1430: An SC-manifold is a closed connected Riemannian manifold for which some L>0 makes every unit-speed geodesic simple closed with length L. Its model CROSS is the compact rank-one symmetric space with the same integral cohomology ring. Rabinowitz loop homology means the degree-shifted Rabinowitz Floer homology of S\*M, with its BV product/operator.

**Source.** Kai Cieliebak, Alexandru Oancea and Egor Shelukhin. *Resonances and string point invertibility for compact rank one symmetric spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.04691v1) Location: Question 3.2, citing Besse Question 7.72.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04691)

<a id="q1520"></a>

## Q1520. Unknotting two glued Seifert surfaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 16

For any oriented link L⊂S³ and Seifert surfaces S₀,S₁ for L, is S₀⁺∪\_L S₁⁻⊂S⁴ smoothly unknotted, where the two interiors are pushed into opposite hemispheres and the union is smoothed along L? Unknotted means bounding a smoothly embedded handlebody.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1520, 1521: Powell proposed Conjecture 2; the cable question already appears in Fehér’s 2023 paper, §1.2, p. 8: https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.12113. Setup for questions 1520, 1521: Seifert surfaces are smooth, compact, connected and oriented. Push their interiors into the indicated 4-ball, keeping the common boundary fixed.

**Source.** Zsombor Fehér. *Non-isotopic Seifert surfaces in the 4-ball and new computational tools for Khovanov homology*. 2025. Advisor(s): András Juhász. [primary source](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid:306ec37f-0583-4a3a-bf19-405b2b58b68b/files/djw827c59x) Location: Conjecture 2, p. 12; §1.2, pp. 9–10.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1520, 1521, checked 6 October 2026: thesis, deposited April 2026, retains both questions; bounded later searches found no resolutions.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.12113) · [2](https://doi.org/10.5287/ora-7rrrxnv0g)

## Q1532. Smooth symplectic curves of high degree

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.1) · **Collection** 16

For every integer d≥18, is every closed connected smooth symplectic surface representing d[CP¹] in CP² symplectically isotopic to a nonsingular complex algebraic curve?

**Context.** Origin for question 1532: Classical smooth symplectic-isotopy problem, restated as Question 1.1; not originated by this paper. Setup for question 1532: Use the standard Fubini–Study symplectic form and standard complex structure on CP². Symplectic isotopy is an isotopy through smooth embedded symplectic surfaces.

**Source.** Marco Golla and Laura Starkston. *The symplectic isotopy problem for rational cuspidal curves*. 2022. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1112/S0010437X2200762X) Location: Question 1.1 and opening discussion, pp. 1595–1596.

## Q1533. Uncountable C⁰ Lagrangian packings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 16

Do there exist a symplectic manifold (M,ω), a closed connected smooth Lagrangian L⊂M and an uncountable family φ\_α∈overline{Ham}(M) such that all φ\_α(L) are smooth submanifolds and are pairwise disjoint?

**Context.** Origin for question 1533: Question 4 in the published version (Question 1.4 in the preprint). Setup for question 1533: Ham(M) consists of time-one maps of compactly supported smooth Hamiltonians; overline{Ham}(M) is its C⁰ closure in Homeo_c(M), as defined by this paper. All manifolds are Hausdorff and second-countable.

**Source.** Joé Brendel, Jean-Philippe Chassé and Laurent Côté. *A note on the cardinality of Lagrangian packings*. 2026. [primary source](https://comptes-rendus.academie-sciences.fr/mathematique/item/10.5802/crmath.835.pdf) Location: Definitions 3 and Question 4, p. 355.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.5802/crmath.835)


<a id="q1628"></a>

## Q1628. Wrapping-squared satellite crossing lower bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

For every satellite knot K=P(C) with nontrivial companion C, is c(K)≥w²c(C)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1628: Satellite Crossing Number Conjecture, restated as K3 Problem 1.2(b); Baker–Motegi (2024) uses this stronger form. Setup for question 1628: A satellite K=P(C) uses a pattern P in a solid torus V, neither a core nor contained in a ball. The wrapping number w minimizes |P∩D| over meridian discs D of V; c is minimal diagram crossing number.

**Source.** Sam Ketchell. *The Crossing Number and Arc Index of Satellite Knots*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.20900v1) Location: Introduction, second paragraph after satellite definition; Theorem 1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1628, checked 6 October 2026: September 17 v1 explicitly treats this as open and proves only c(K)≥(2/5)(w²c(C))^(1/11).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20900)


<a id="q1629"></a>

## Q1629. Stable crossing growth under twisting

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

For every such twist family, does lim_{n→∞}c(K_n)/n exist and equal η(η−1)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1629, 1630: The authors formulate both stable-limit conjectures; the braid question cites related work of Nutt (1999), Conjecture 3.12. Setup for questions 1629, 1630: K_n is obtained from K⊂S³ by n full twists about a disjoint unknot c. The wrapping number η≥2 is minimal geometric intersection with a spanning disc of c; ω=|lk(K,c)|. c(K) and b(K) denote crossing number and braid index.

**Source.** Kenneth L. Baker and Kimihiko Motegi. *The stable crossing number of a twist family of knots and the satellite crossing number conjecture*. 2024. [primary source](https://journals.sns.it/index.php/annaliscienze/article/download/6678/2106/3651) Location: Stable Crossing Number Conjecture, introduction, preprint p. 3; journal p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1629, 1630, checked 6 October 2026: publisher version retains both conjectures; coherent and certain wrapping-two families are proved, not the general cases.

## Q1631. Characterizing Liouville manifolds by their skeleton

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.3) · **Collection** 17

Let (V⁴,dλ,λ) be a finite-type Liouville manifold whose skeleton is a codimension-one C¹ submanifold, normally hyperbolic for the Liouville flow, and carries the smooth oriented Anosov flow Φ induced by that flow. Must it be exact symplectomorphic to an AL structure supporting Φ?

**Context.** Origin for question 1631: Massoni’s conjecture. Setup for question 1631: An AL structure for a smooth oriented Anosov flow Φ on a closed connected oriented 3-manifold M is λ=e^(−s)α₋+e^sα₊ on R×M, where α₋,α₊ form a transverse negative/positive Liouville pair supporting Φ; thus dλ is symplectic and kerα₋∩kerα₊ is the oriented flow line field. Finite type means cylindrical outside a compact set; the skeleton is the Liouville nonescaping set.

**Source.** Thomas Massoni. *A symplectic viewpoint on Anosov flows*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2503.00123v2) Location: Conjecture 6.3; following paragraph distinguishes C¹-strict equivalence.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1631, checked 6 October 2026: renewed primary-source and exact-topic checks found no resolution; bounded-negative search, not exhaustive verification.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15592)

## Q1718. Large nondifferentiability sets preserving smooth curves

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 18

Can an element of Homeo¹(S) have an uncountable set of nondifferentiability points, or one of positive area?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1718, 1719: Containment: thesis §1.6. Nondifferentiability: Booth’s 2024 preprint, §1.3, Question 1. Setup for questions 1718, 1719: For a smooth surface S and k∈{1,2,…,∞}, Homeo^k(S) consists of homeomorphisms f such that f and f⁻¹ send every embedded regular C^k circle to an embedded regular C^k circle. Differentiability is in smooth coordinates; measure means any smooth area measure.

**Source.** Katherine Williams Booth. *Automorphisms of Smooth Fine Curve Graphs*. 2025. Advisor(s): Dan Margalit. [primary source](https://repository.gatech.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/1518c5d1-9c3b-4271-ba33-acf4f5a41247/content) Location: §1.6, Question 3, p. 7 (PDF p. 20).

## Q1720. Bounded-sequence space inside a fine curve subgraph

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.5) · **Collection** 18

Does some C†\_A(S_{g,b}) admit a quasi-isometric embedding of ℓ∞?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1720, 1721: Question 1.5 is attributed to Alex Wright; Question 1.6 is the authors’ question. Setup for questions 1720, 1721: S_{g,b} is a compact orientable surface with 3g+b>4. The fine curve graph has actual essential nonperipheral embedded circles as vertices and disjointness edges, with unit-edge metric. C†\_A is induced by isotopy classes A forming a connected subgraph of the ordinary curve graph; C†\_α means A={[α]}. ℓ∞ is the space of bounded real sequences with supremum norm.

**Source.** Ryan Dickmann and Roberta Shapiro. *Large flats in large subgraphs of fine curve graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.25086v1) Location: Question 1.5, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1720, 1721, checked 6 October 2026: the current paper proves c₀₀ embeddings, retaining both questions; bounded later searches found no answers.

## Q1819. Antisymmetry of infinite-tangle embedding

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Does ℓ≼ℓ′≼ℓ imply that ℓ and ℓ′ are ambiently isotopic?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1819, 1820, 1821: Thesis §6.3. Setup for questions 1819, 1820, 1821: I=[−1,1], M=R×I². A Z-tangle is a smooth proper embedding Z×I→M, transverse to ∂M, sending (n,±1) to (n,±1,0). Isotopies fix ∂M. Write ℓ≼ℓ′ if ψℓ=ℓ′ for a boundary-fixing embedding ψ:M→M, not necessarily proper. Weak isotopy means a smooth jointly proper family whose slices are Z-tangles. Finite isotopy means isotopy on every finite set of strands.

**Source.** Larsen Drew Linov. *Properties of Knotoids and Z-Tangles*. 2024. Advisor(s): Ian Agol. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt8jn6v4gj/qt8jn6v4gj.pdf) Location: §6.3, p. 67.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1819, 1820, 1821, checked 6 October 2026: bounded later searches found no resolutions.

**Further links.** [1](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/8jn6v4gj)

## Q1822. Stein lasagna nonvanishing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.5.19) · **Collection** 19

Is S²₀(X) nonzero for every Stein 2-handlebody X?

**Context.** Origin for question 1822: Ren–Willis (2024), retained as thesis Conjecture 3.5.19. Setup for question 1822: S²₀(X) is the integral gl₂ Khovanov skein lasagna module with empty boundary link: framed oriented surfaces in X minus finitely many input four-balls, decorated by integral Khovanov classes on their boundary links, modulo multilinearity and replacement by Khovanov cobordism maps. A Stein 2-handlebody has a finite Stein handle decomposition using handles of indices ≤2.

**Source.** Qiuyu Ren. *Khovanov homology and smooth 4-manifolds*. 2026. Advisor(s): Ian Agol. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt3x47z3d7/qt3x47z3d7.pdf) Location: Conjecture 3.5.19, p. 114.

<a id="q1829"></a>

## Q1829. Symplectization rigidity in dimension three

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.1) · **Collection** 19

Can two such contact manifolds fail to be contactomorphic while their symplectizations are symplectomorphic, without requiring the symplectomorphism to be exact?

**Context.** Origin for question 1829: McDuff–Salamon, Introduction to Symplectic Topology, third edition (2017), Problem 40; restated as Question 1.1. Setup for question 1829: Contact manifolds are closed, connected, oriented three-manifolds with positive cooriented contact structures ξ=kerα. Their symplectizations are (R×Y,d(eᵗα)); the symplectic type is independent of the positive defining form.

**Source.** Nobuo Iida. *The Three-Dimensional Symplectization Question for Small Seifert L-Spaces and Further Classes*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.29534) Location: Question 1.1, §1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1829, checked 6 October 2026: many specified three-manifold classes are settled; the general question remains.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29534)


<a id="q1830"></a>

## Q1830. Strong-cobordism contact naturality

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.4) · **Collection** 19

Must F̂\_{W†,k}(c(ξ₂))=c(ξ₁) hold for every strong symplectic cobordism W?

**Context.** Origin for question 1830: Question 1.4, following the Stein naturality theorem. Setup for question 1830: Use hat Heegaard Floer homology over F₂ and its Ozsváth–Szabó contact class. A compact strong symplectic cobordism W:(Y₁,ξ₁)→(Y₂,ξ₂) has concave incoming and convex outgoing contact-type boundary. Choose basepoints and a joining path for the hat cobordism map. W† runs from −Y₂ to −Y₁; k is the symplectic canonical Spinᶜ structure.

**Source.** Matthew Hedden and Lev Tovstopyat-Nelip. *On naturality of the Ozsváth–Szabó contact invariant*. 2022. [primary source](https://msp.org/obs/2022/5-1/obs-v5-n1-p06-p.pdf) Location: Question 1.4, p. 126; conventions Theorem 1.3, p. 125.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1830, checked 6 October 2026: Iida’s 2026 paper explicitly reaffirms that strong-cobordism naturality remains open.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.29534)

## Q2019. Sharp high-exponent chord maximizers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 21

For each finite p>2, determine all convex planar closed curves maximizing D_p(γ)/L, up to similarity and reparametrization.

**Context.** Origin for questions 2019, 2020: Questions 6.1,6.3; both present in v3. Setup for questions 2019, 2020: For a closed rectifiable curve γ of length L, D_p=(L⁻²∫γ∫γ|x−y|^p ds_x ds_y)^(1/p); D₀ uses the geometric mean and D∞ the diameter. Thickness is the maximal embedded normal-tube radius; Rop(γ)=L/thickness and Rop(K)=inf_{γ∈K}Rop(γ). Put ρ\_{p,λ}(K)=inf{L/D_p:γ∈K is embedded C¹,¹, Rop(γ)≤λRop(K)}, λ≥1. Convex curves include rectifiable limits such as a doubled segment.

**Source.** Makoto Ozawa. *Ropelength-Filtered Knot Densities*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23621v4) Location: Question 6.1, v4 p. 26; v3 p. 32.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2019, 2020, checked 6 October 2026: retained October 3; knot-type independence and right-continuity are proved.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23621v3)

## Q2021. Exact complexity of exotic R⁴

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6) · **Collection** 21

Determine the Borel-bireducibility degree of diffeomorphism among smooth manifolds homeomorphic to R⁴.

**Context.** Origin for questions 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024: §9; R⁴ question follows Foreman (2018). Setup for questions 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024: Use §3 atlas spaces. Borel reducibility E≤\_B F requires Borel f with xEy⇔f(x)Ff(y). Manifolds are connected, second-countable, boundaryless; hyperbolic ones are complete and oriented, with orientation-preserving isometries. Algebraically finite means π₁ finitely generated.

**Source.** Jeffrey Bergfalk, Iian B. Smythe. *Manifold classification from the descriptive viewpoint*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24996) Location: §9 Question 6, p. 102.

## Q2027. Eventual low-tb destabilization

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 21

For each null-homologous smooth knot type K in a tight contact manifold, is there n∈Z such that every Legendrian L∈K with tb(L)&lt;n is S₊(L′) or S₋(L′) for some Legendrian L′?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2027, 2028: Conjecture 1.1 and Question 1.3; first also in Etnyre’s 2025 survey. Setup for questions 2027, 2028: Contact manifolds have dimension three. A Legendrian knot is non-loose when its ambient contact structure is overtwisted but its standard-neighborhood exterior is tight. S₊,S₋ are positive/negative Legendrian stabilization; tb is the Thurston–Bennequin invariant relative to a Seifert surface.

**Source.** John B. Etnyre. *Neighborhoods of transverse knots and destabilizations*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15651v2) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 1.

## Q2221. Asphericity of holomorphic strata

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 23

For every g≥2 and μ, is the orbifold universal cover of each connected component of H(μ) contractible?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2221, 2222, 2223: Asphericity: Kontsevich–Zorich 1997; affineness: Chen; residueless: Problem 3.4. Setup for questions 2221, 2222, 2223: H(μ) parametrizes nonzero holomorphic one-forms on smooth complex genus-g curves with distinct labelled zeros of positive orders μ partitioning 2g−2; PH(μ)=H(μ)/C\*.

**Source.** Dawei Chen; Fei Yu. *An algebro-geometric perspective on the topology of moduli spaces of differentials*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13127) Location: §4.1, pp. 10–11.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2221, 2222, 2223, checked 6 October 2026: no resolutions located. June non-asphericity examples require poles; holomorphic case remains open.

## Q2418. Finite normal generation of equivariant-isotopy kernels

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For n≥3, is ker(P_G) always the normal closure of a finite subset in Γ\_G?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2418, 2419: Lucas, Problem 1.2 and Question 1.3; higher-homotopy predecessor in his 2025 paper. Setup for questions 2418, 2419: M is a closed oriented smooth n-manifold; G is a finite group of orientation-preserving diffeomorphisms. Diff⁺(M)^G denotes the centralizer, Γ\_G=π₀(Diff⁺(M)^G), and P_G:Γ\_G→π₀(Diff⁺(M)) forgets equivariance.

**Source.** Trent Lucas. *Isotopy versus equivariant isotopy in dimensions three and higher*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11104) Location: Question 1.3, p. 6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2418, 2419, checked 6 October 2026: September 23 v3 retains both; no later resolution located.

## Q2420. Injectivity of unbranched lifting maps

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.6) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Must the lifting homomorphism L_p:LMod_p(N)→SMod_p(M)/Γ be injective?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2420: see question 2418. Origin for question 2420: Lucas, Question 1.6, following Margalit–Winarski’s Birman–Hilden program. Setup for question 2420: For a finite unbranched covering p:M→N of closed oriented connected 3-manifolds, LMod_p(N) consists of orientation-preserving mapping classes that lift. SMod_p(M) consists of classes with fiber-preserving representatives; Γ is the image of the deck group in Mod(M).

**Source.** Trent Lucas. *Birman-Hilden theory for 3-manifolds*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07798) Location: Question 1.6, p. 6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2420, checked 6 October 2026: v3 and the September 2026 sequel leave the general unbranched case open.

<a id="q2421"></a>

## Q2421. Normal generation by Veech cylinder cores

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.7) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For which Veech surfaces does the normal closure in π₁(X) of the core curves of every cylinder decomposition equal π₁(X)? Equivalently, when does f induce π₁(Y)≅π₁(B)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2421, 2422: Freedman–Lucas, Questions 4.7 and 5.2; fibrations originate with Möller (2005). Setup for questions 2421, 2422: A Veech surface (X,ω) is a closed translation surface whose SL₂(R)-orbit projects to a Teichmüller curve. Pull its tautological family back to a finite torsion-free manifold cover of moduli space and take the smooth semistable compactification f:Y→B; this is a Veech fibration.

**Source.** Sam Freedman; Trent Lucas. *Veech fibrations*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.02325) Location: Question 4.7, p. 13.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2421, 2422, checked 6 October 2026: November 2025 v3 retains both; no later answer located.

**Further links.** [1](https://trent-lucas.github.io/documents/Veech_fibrations.pdf)

## Q2427. Local-knot slice genus in larger three-manifolds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Is g_{Y×I}(K)=g₄(K) for every such local knot K and Y, where g₄ is the smooth slice genus in B⁴?

**Context.** Origin for question 2427: Earlier local-knot genus question, including Klug–Ruppik (2021), §2; reiterated p. 2. Setup for question 2427: For K in a ball in a smooth oriented 3-manifold Y, g_{Y×I}(K) is the minimum genus of compact connected oriented surfaces properly smoothly embedded in Y×[0,1] with boundary exactly K×{1}. View K also as a knot in S³.

**Source.** Junghwan Park; Zhongtao Wu; Jingling Yang. *Local Knots, ν⁺-Sharp Knots, and Rational Slice Genus*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18619) Location: Introduction, p. 2, before Theorem 1.1.

<a id="q2428"></a>

## Q2428. Coherence, L²-homology and nonpositive immersions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For every group G of geometric dimension at most two, are these equivalent: G is coherent; b₂^(2)(G)=0; and G≅π₁(X) for some 2-complex X with nonpositive immersions?

**Context.** Origin for question 2428: Wise’s nonpositive-immersion program; Fisher–Sánchez-Peralta Conjecture 1.1, with Linton appendix. Setup for question 2428: Coherent means every finitely generated subgroup is finitely presented. Geometric dimension≤2 means a K(G,1) of dimension≤2 exists. A 2-complex X has nonpositive immersions if every cellular immersion of a compact connected 2-complex Y into X satisfies χ(Y)≤0 or π₁(Y)=1.

**Source.** Sam P. Fisher; Pablo Sánchez-Peralta, with an appendix by Marco Linton. *Coherent RFRS groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16763) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2428, checked 6 October 2026: virtually RFRS partial results leave this unrestricted conjecture; the nonpositive-immersion conclusion is only virtual.

## Q2516. Asymptotic scarcity of fake-surface spines

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.2) · **Collection** 26

Among contractible cellular fake surfaces of complexity n, does the fraction that occur as spines of compact 3-manifolds tend to zero as n→∞? A spine is a subpolyhedron onto which the manifold collapses.

**Context.** Origin for questions 2516, 2517: Joint work with Yang Qiu and Zhenghan Wang, first circulated in 2024. Setup for questions 2516, 2517: A cellular fake surface is a compact polyhedron locally modelled on a disk, three half-disks sharing their diameter, or the cone on K₄, with disk-shaped intrinsic strata. Its complexity is its number of cone-on-K₄ vertices. Count surfaces up to PL homeomorphism.

**Source.** Lucas Fagan. *The Stable Andrews–Curtis Conjecture and Generic 2-Polyhedra*. 2025. Advisor(s): Zhenghan Wang. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt6fm617h4/qt6fm617h4.pdf?v=lg) Location: Conjecture 5.2, p. 95.

## Q2520. Local connectedness of weighted multicurves

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.2.3) · **Collection** 26

Is M locally connected in the subspace topology inherited from PML?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2520, 2521: Esmee Riet te Winkel. PhD, University of Warwick, 2020. Advisors: Brian H. Bowditch; Saul Schleimer. Origin for questions 2520, 2521: Both questions are posed by te Winkel in Chapter 7. Setup for questions 2520, 2521: Fix a complete finite-area hyperbolic metric on S₀,₅. PML is its projective measured-lamination space. Let M⊂PML consist of projective measures supported on one or two disjoint essential simple closed geodesics. Let BL consist of compact minimal geodesic laminations with more than one leaf, with the coarse-Hausdorff (superconvergence) topology: λ\_i→λ when every Hausdorff-convergent subsequence has limit containing λ.

**Source.** Esmee Riet te Winkel. *Curves and laminations on the five-times punctured sphere*. 2020. Advisor(s): Brian Bowditch and Saul Schleimer. [primary source](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/158340/1/WRAP_Theses_teWinkel_2020.pdf) Location: Question 7.2.3, p. 99; Definition 4.1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2520, 2521, checked 7 October 2026: No later resolution located in targeted searches. The thesis proves the dimension is either one or two.

**Further links.** [1](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/158340/)

## Q2523. Two triangles with no connecting dome

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.13) · **Collection** 26

Do there exist two disjoint, positioned equilateral unit-triangle boundary curves in R³ whose union bounds no dome?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2523, 2524: Glazyrin–Pak, Domes over curves (2020 preprint; 2022 publication), Conjectures 5.13 and 5.14; retained in Miranda’s thesis. Setup for questions 2523, 2524: An integral curve is a closed polygonal curve in R³ with unit-length edges. A spatial unit rhombus is such a four-edge curve, not necessarily planar. A dome is a realization in R³ of a pure connected finite 2-dimensional simplicial complex whose facets are equilateral unit triangles and whose boundary is the prescribed curve union. Embedding, immersion and orientability are not required.

**Source.** Robert Miranda. *Rigidity and Flexibility of Polyhedral Structures*. 2025. Advisor(s): Igor Pak. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt92k2r4mv/qt92k2r4mv.pdf) Location: §1.5.1, p. 16; cited Conjecture 5.13.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2523, 2524, checked 7 October 2026: The orientable single-rhombus version is false. Thesis and February 2025 paper retain the stated problems; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/92k2r4mv) · [2](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~pak/papers/Miranda-thesis.pdf) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02517) · [4](https://arxiv.org/html/2408.02517v2) · [5](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~pak/papers/K-curve.pdf) · [6](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.02555)

<a id="q2531"></a>

## Q2531. Hardness of finding a noncollapsible 2-tree

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Is deciding whether K contains a 2-tree that is not 2-collapsible NP-hard?

**Context.** Origin for question 2531: Questions posed in the thesis’s original Chapter 5. Setup for question 2531: Let K be a finite simplicial complex, represented by its simplices, and ∂₂ its F₂ edge–triangle boundary matrix. A 2-tree is a set of linearly independent columns; it is 2-collapsible if repeatedly removing a triangle incident to a free edge removes all its triangles. A 1-cotree is a set of independent rows; it is cocollapsible if repeatedly selecting a column with exactly one remaining selected row and deleting that row removes all selected rows.

**Source.** Ondrej Draganov. *Structures and computations in topological data analysis*. 2025. Advisor(s): Herbert Edelsbrunner. [primary source](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/download/18979/19000/Thesis.pdf) Location: §5.5, final paragraph, p. 126; definitions p. 116.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2531, checked 7 October 2026: Targeted searches located no resolution; this is a bounded negative check, not a current reaffirmation.

**Further links.** [1](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/record/18979)

## Q2621. Truncated detection of Kervaire spheres

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.1) · **Collection** 27

For which m≥2 and integers 2≤k<∞ are E_(Σ\_K^(4m+1)) and E_(S^(4m+1)) equivalent on Disc⁺\_(4m+1,≤k)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2621, 2622: Paper B, joint with Manuel Krannich and Alexander Kupers. Setup for questions 2621, 2622: Disc⁺\_d is the ∞-category of finite disjoint unions of R^d with orientation-preserving smooth embedding spaces in the C∞ topology. Set E_M(U)=Emb⁺(U,M), as a space-valued presheaf. Disc⁺\_(d,≤k) restricts to unions with at most k components. Σ\_K^(4m+1) is the Kervaire sphere, the boundary of the plumbing of two tangent disk bundles of S^(2m+1).

**Source.** Fadi Mezher. *Embedding calculus and automorphisms of high dimensional manifolds*. 2025. Advisor(s): Søren Galatius and Nathalie Wahl. [primary source](https://www.math.ku.dk/bibliotek/arkivet/phd-theses/phd25fm.pdf) Location: Paper B, Question 1.1, p. 3 (PDF p. 79).

## Q2625. Completeness of symmetric probes

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 27

If T(x) and T(y) are Hamiltonian isotopic in X, must x and y be connected by finitely many symmetric-probe moves?

**Context.** Origin for question 2625: Brendel’s symmetric-probe completeness conjecture. Setup for question 2625: Let (X^(2n),ω,μ) be a toric symplectic manifold with moment image Δ, and T(x)=μ⁻¹(x) for x∈int Δ. A symmetric probe is a rational segment in Δ meeting its boundary in the relative interiors of two facets, with the primitive segment direction pairing to ±1 with each primitive facet normal. A probe move exchanges points equidistant from its endpoints in integral-affine length.

**Source.** Joé Brendel. *Hamiltonian classification of toric fibres and symmetric probes*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.00334) Location: Conjecture 1.3; journal p. 1841.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2625, checked 7 October 2026: Final June 2025 text retains the conjecture; no later general resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://msp.org/agt/2025/25-3/agt-v25-n3-p.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.00334)

## Q2626. Sharp contact-surgery overhead

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 27

Do there exist (M,ξ),(N,η) with cs_±1((M,ξ),(N,η))=s_Z(M,N)+5?

**Context.** Origin for question 2626: The authors ask whether the additive constant five in Theorem 1.1 is sharp. Setup for question 2626: For closed connected oriented 3-manifolds with positive coorientable contact structures, cs_±1 is the least number of components of a Legendrian link giving the other manifold by contact ±1 surgeries, up to contactomorphism. Let s_Z(M,N) be the analogous minimum for integral smooth Dehn surgery, up to oriented diffeomorphism.

**Source.** Marc Kegel, Isacco Nonino, Monika Yadav. *Contact surgery distance*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2512.14904v1) Location: Introduction, immediately after Theorem 1.1, p. 2.

## Q2627. Detection beyond transverse simplicity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.8) · **Collection** 27

Does some transversely nonsimple smooth knot type K have the property that every transverse realization T of K is determined by C_n(T) for some n≥2, allowed to depend on T?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2627, 2628: Questions of Kegel–Nonino. Setup for questions 2627, 2628: All transverse knots lie in the standard tight contact S³ and are positively oriented. C_n(T) denotes the cyclic n-fold branched cover with its canonical contact structure. T is determined by C_n(T) if every transverse knot with contactomorphic C_n is transversely isotopic to T. A smooth knot type is transversely simple if smooth type and self-linking number determine transverse isotopy.

**Source.** Marc Kegel, Isacco Nonino. *Transverse knots determined by their cyclic branched covers*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.25805v1) Location: Question 1.8, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2627, 2628, checked 7 October 2026: March 2026 preprint retains both questions; no subsequent resolution located.

## Q2629. Tight partial open books from quasipositive links

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.10) · **Collection** 27

If Σ’s associated contact structure is tight, is the same abstract partial open book realized by a Seifert surface of some strongly quasipositive link in S³?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2629, 2630: Questions posed after the nonfibered counterexamples. Setup for questions 2629, 2630: For an incompressible Seifert surface Σ⊂S³, let Σ′ be the union of all arcs admitting product disks in its cut-open complement. Choose a basis B of Σ′ with endpoints on ∂Σ and let P≠∅ be its neighborhood; each disk identifies an arc a with h(a). Use Definition 3.1’s partial open book (Σ,P,h) and its Honda–Kazez–Matić sutured Floer contact invariant. A strongly quasipositive link bounds a subsurface of a positive-torus-knot fiber surface.

**Source.** Isacco Nonino, Miguel Orbegozo Rodriguez. *Non-fibered strongly quasipositive links and tightness*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.26183v1) Location: Question 3.10, p. 7.

## Q2634. Planar Rips complexes are wedges of spheres

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 27

Whenever VR_r(P) is connected, is it homotopy equivalent to a finite, possibly empty, wedge of spheres?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2634, 2635: Problems on geometric complexes and Houghton groups in Zaremsky’s list. Setup for questions 2634, 2635: For finite P⊂R² and r>0, VR_r(P) has simplices the subsets of Euclidean diameter≤r. H_n is the group of permutations of {1,…,n}×N that are eventually translations on each ray. Its Dehn function records the maximal minimal van Kampen area of null words of length≤L, up to the usual coarse equivalence.

**Source.** Matthew C. B. Zaremsky. *Some open problems, version dated 12 July 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://zaremsky.github.io/open_problems.pdf) Location: §4, Problem 2, p. 4.

<a id="q2723"></a>

## Q2723. Scott squares of sober countable dcpos

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

For every countable meet-continuous dcpo P with sober Scott space, is σ(P×P)=σ(P)×σ(P), where the right side is the ordinary product topology?

**Context.** Origin for question 2723: Question posed after the paper’s counterexamples. Setup for question 2723: A dcpo is a nonempty poset in which every nonempty directed subset has a supremum. Its Scott topology σ has upward-closed opens inaccessible by directed suprema. It is meet-continuous when x≤sup D implies x∈clσ(↓x∩↓D). Its Scott space is sober when every nonempty irreducible closed set is ↓x for a unique x.

**Source.** Xiaoquan Xu, Wei Ji. *Scott products and sobriety of countable meet-continuous dcpos*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.02642) Location: §6, p. 16.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2723, checked 7 October 2026: Explicitly open in the October 2026 primary preprint; no later resolution located.


<a id="q2724"></a>

## Q2724. Core-compact meet-continuous dcpos are sober

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.5.8) · **Collection** 28

Must every meet-continuous dcpo whose Scott space is core-compact have a sober Scott space?

**Context.** Origin for question 2724: Jia’s doctoral question, not the separately posed function-space variant. Setup for question 2724: A dcpo is a nonempty poset in which every nonempty directed subset has a supremum. Its Scott topology σ has upward-closed opens inaccessible by directed suprema. It is meet-continuous when x≤sup D implies x∈clσ(↓x∩↓D). Its Scott space is sober when every nonempty irreducible closed set is ↓x for a unique x. Core-compact means that the lattice of open sets is a continuous lattice.

**Source.** Xiaodong Jia. *Meet-continuity and locally compact sober dcpos*. 2018. Advisor(s): Achim Jung. [primary source](https://etheses.bham.ac.uk/id/eprint/8085/1/Jia18PhD.pdf) Location: Question 4.5.8, p. 139.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2724, checked 7 October 2026: October 2026 work retains the unrestricted question; countable and L-dcpo cases are solved.

**Further links.** [1](https://etheses.bham.ac.uk/id/eprint/8085/) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02642) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24001)

## Q2730. Exact folded ribbonlength of the figure-eight

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 12) · **Collection** 28

Is Rib([4₁])=8 for the figure-eight knot?

**Context.** Origin for question 2730: Authors’ explicit figure-eight conjecture. Setup for question 2730: A folded ribbon is a constant-width ribbon around a polygonal knot diagram, folded at its vertices, with continuous over/under data consistent with its crossings; both annular and Möbius ribbons are allowed. Its ribbonlength is the polygonal core length divided by width. Rib([K]) is the infimum over all such ribbons of knot type K.

**Source.** Zhicheng Chen, Elizabeth Denne, Kyle Patterson, Timi Patterson. *Ribbonlength upper bounds for small crossing knots and links*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.16190) Location: Conjecture 12, p. 13; definitions pp. 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2730, checked 7 October 2026: The current paper attains 8; bounded later searches found no matching lower bound or counterexample.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16190v1)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 28

For every alternating knot K, are all c(a)>0, does A=conv(A)∩Z², and does log c extend to a concave function on conv(A)?

**Source.** Stavros Garoufalidis, Shana Yunsheng Li, Josephine Yu. *Positivity and log concavity of the Links–Gould polynomial of knots*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.30754) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 3; definitions p. 2.

## Q2733. Nonorientable regular manifold covers of hyperbolic orbifolds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.10) · **Collection** 28

Must every proper NEC group have a torsion-free finite-index normal subgroup containing an orientation-reversing element?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2733, 2734: The authors’ NEC extension and alternating-group realization question. Setup for questions 2733, 2734: A proper NEC group is a discrete cocompact subgroup of Isom(H²) containing orientation-reversing elements. A regular map is a cellular graph embedding in a closed surface whose automorphism group is transitive on incident vertex-edge-face flags; type {m,k} means m-gonal faces and valency k.

**Source.** Emilio Bujalance, Francisco Javier Cirre, Marston D. E. Conder, Antonio F. Costa. *Fenchel’s conjecture on NEC groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s13163-025-00540-w) Location: Introduction p. 313; remaining cases Question 4.10, p. 326.

Write br(K) for bridge number, σ\_a(F_K) for the a-exponent span of the ambient-isotopy Kauffman polynomial, and deg_z(P_K) for HOMFLY–PT z-degree, with P_U=1 and aP_+−a⁻¹P_−=zP_0. Use Ozsváth–Szabó τ over F₂ and Rasmussen s over Q.

**Source.** Michał Jabłonowski. *Integer Knot Invariants: Inequalities, Computations, and Open Problems, September 2026 version*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22652v5) Location: §5, candidate (c9), p. 20.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2823, 2824, 2825, checked 7 October 2026: The September 15 version retains all three; its partial results do not establish universal validity. No later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22652) · [2](https://mat.ug.edu.pl/old/doktoraty/doktoraty-2011/)

## Q2826. One common extreme-type de Rham class

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 11.1) · **Collection** 29

For every even n≥4, does a connected compact complex n-manifold X exist with dim_C V_{n−1}(X)=1?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2826, 2827: Jonas Robin Stelzig. Double Complexes and Hodge Structures as Vector Bundles. PhD, University of Münster, 2018. Advisors: Christopher Deninger; Jörg Schürmann. Origin for questions 2826, 2827: Stelzig’s primary research problems, reaffirmed in his 2025 survey. Setup for questions 2826, 2827: For a compact complex n-manifold X, E_r^{p,q}(X) denotes its Frölicher spectral sequence, with d_r:E_r^{p,q}→E_r^{p+r,q−r+1}. Let V_k(X)⊂H^k_dR(X;C) be the classes admitting both a d-closed holomorphic k-form representative and a d-closed antiholomorphic k-form representative.

**Source.** Jonas Stelzig. *On linear combinations of cohomological invariants of compact complex manifolds*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.08419v2) Location: Problem 11.1, p. 40; 2025 survey Problem 2.10.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2826, 2827, checked 7 October 2026: The 2025 article retains both constructions; no later resolution located.

## Q2828. Zero Euler class and genuine plat presentation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

If every component F of a surface-link has normal Euler number e(F)=0, must the link be isotopic to the plat closure of some two-dimensional braid?

**Context.** Origin for question 2828: Yasuda’s explicit converse question. Setup for question 2828: Surface-links are smoothly embedded closed surfaces in R⁴, possibly nonorientable. Use Yasuda’s plat closure of an even-degree two-dimensional braid: a simple branched covering S⊂D²×D² under the second projection, with product boundary, closed by the standard wicket caps.

**Source.** Jumpei Yasuda. *On the classification of 2-plat 2-knots, May 2026 version*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.15401v2) Location: Remark 2.6, p. 5.

<a id="q2829"></a>

## Q2829. Grigorchuk’s Dehn spectrum

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.3) · **Collection** 29

For the first Grigorchuk group G, generated on the binary rooted tree by a=(1,1)σ, b=(a,c), c=(a,d), d=(1,b), with σ swapping root branches, is f_G(k,m,n)∼(n/m)²?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2829, 2830: Osin–Rybak question. Setup for questions 2829, 2830: For G=F(X)/K with finite X, put R_k={w∈K:|w|≤k} and f_G(k,m,n)=max_{w∈⟨⟨R_k⟩⟩,|w|≤n}Area_{R_m}(w), m≥k; area is the minimum number of conjugates in F(X) of relators or inverses whose product is w. Here k,m,n are positive integers and |w| is word length. Define f≼g by ∃C∈N_{>0} ∀k,m,n with m≥Ck: f(k,Cm,n)≤Cg(Ck,m,Cn)+Cn/m+C; ∼ means mutual ≼. Also B(r,N)=F_r/⟨⟨w^N:w∈F_r⟩⟩.

**Source.** Denis Osin, Ekaterina Rybak. *Isoperimetric inequalities in finitely generated groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.12518v4) Location: Problem 6.3 and following paragraph, p. 49.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2829, 2830, checked 7 October 2026: The 2026 version retains both targets; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00209-026-03992-y) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/ekaterinarybak/home)

## Q2830. Quasi-isometry and Burnside exponent parity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.5) · **Collection** 29

For every fixed r,s≥2, does N₀(r,s) exist such that B(r,M) and B(s,N) are not quasi-isometric whenever M≥N₀ is even and N≥N₀ is odd?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2829, 2830: Osin–Rybak question. Setup for questions 2829, 2830: For G=F(X)/K with finite X, put R_k={w∈K:|w|≤k} and f_G(k,m,n)=max_{w∈⟨⟨R_k⟩⟩,|w|≤n}Area_{R_m}(w), m≥k; area is the minimum number of conjugates in F(X) of relators or inverses whose product is w. Here k,m,n are positive integers and |w| is word length. Define f≼g by ∃C∈N_{>0} ∀k,m,n with m≥Ck: f(k,Cm,n)≤Cg(Ck,m,Cn)+Cn/m+C; ∼ means mutual ≼. Also B(r,N)=F_r/⟨⟨w^N:w∈F_r⟩⟩.

**Source.** Denis Osin, Ekaterina Rybak. *Isoperimetric inequalities in finitely generated groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.12518v4) Location: Problem 6.5, p. 50, particular even/odd question.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2829, 2830, checked 7 October 2026: The 2026 version retains both targets; no later resolution located.

<a id="q2832"></a>

## Q2832. Frattini orbits without countability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.1) · **Collection** 29

For every general-type subgroup G≤Isom(S), with no countability assumption, must every Φ(G)-orbit in S be bounded?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2832, 2833: Gil Goffer. On invariable generation in infinite groups and on groups of almost automorphisms of trees. PhD, Weizmann Institute of Science, 2023. Advisors: Tsachik Gelander. Origin for questions 2832, 2833: Goffer–Osin–Rybak questions. Setup for questions 2832, 2833: Let S be a geodesic Gromov-hyperbolic space. An isometric G-action has general type if its limit set has more than two points and G fixes no boundary point. Φ(G) is the intersection of maximal proper subgroups (G if none). A group is invariably generated if some A⊆G generates after independently conjugating every a∈A by an arbitrary g_a∈G.

**Source.** Gil Goffer, Denis Osin, Ekaterina Rybak. *Frattini subgroups of hyperbolic-like groups*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.04592v3) Location: Problem 4.1(a), p. 13.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2832, 2833, checked 7 October 2026: The countable Frattini case is proved. Later boundary-dynamics results do not settle these remaining questions; no resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jalgebra.2024.05.043) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14159) · [3](https://ppfp.ucop.edu/info/fellowship-recipients/fellows-pages/goffer-gil.html)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.2) · **Collection** 29

Does there exist an invariably generated group admitting a general-type action on a hyperbolic space?

**Source.** Gil Goffer, Denis Osin, Ekaterina Rybak. *Frattini subgroups of hyperbolic-like groups*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.04592v3) Location: Problem 4.2, p. 14, first question.

## Q2834. Minimizing generators for surface-group growth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.1) · **Collection** 29

For each g≥2, explicitly determine a finite generating set S_g with e(G_g,S_g)=e(G_g).

**Context.** Origin for question 2834: Fujiwara’s problem; existence of a minimizing set is already known. Setup for question 2834: Let G_g=π₁(Σ\_g), where Σ\_g is a closed orientable surface of genus g≥2. For a finite generating set S, put e(G_g,S)=lim_{n→∞}|B_S(n)|^(1/n), where B_S(n) consists of products of at most n elements of S∪S⁻¹. Set e(G_g)=inf_S e(G_g,S).

**Source.** Koji Fujiwara. *contribution to Problems on Low-dimensional Topology, 2025, edited by Tomotada Ohtsuki*. 2025. [primary source](https://www.kurims.kyoto-u.ac.jp/~ildt/prob25.pdf) Location: Problem 4.1, p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2834, checked 7 October 2026: The 2025 problem remains unresolved in bounded later searches; no minimizing-set classification located.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.math.kyoto-u.ac.jp/~kfujiwara/)

## Q2925. Finite colour counts distinguish links

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 30

If L and L′ are not related by isotopy and reversed mirroring of split summands, must some finite quandle Q satisfy |Hom(Q_L,Q)|≠|Hom(Q_{L′},Q)|?

**Context.** Origin for question 2925: Darné’s link form of the Clark–Elhamdadi–Saito–Yeatman knot conjecture. Setup for question 2925: Links are oriented smooth links in S³. Reversed mirroring means mirroring and simultaneously reversing all orientations in any chosen nonsplit summand of the split-link decomposition. A finite-quandle colouring is a homomorphism from the fundamental quandle Q_L to a finite quandle.

**Source.** Jacques Darné. *Profinite rigidity and quandle colourings*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.06485v1) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 1; Theorem A, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2925, checked 7 October 2026: The 5 October primary paper explicitly conjectures this, proving an equivalent profinite formulation rather than resolving it.


<a id="q2926"></a>

## Q2926. A subadditive partner for every knot

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.1) · **Collection** 30

For every nontrivial knot K, does some knot K′ satisfy u(K#K′)&lt;u(K)+u(K′)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2926, 2927: Universal symbiont conjecture: Brittenham–Hermiller. The 10₆ computation is an older unresolved table entry revisited here. Setup for questions 2926, 2927: For a smooth knot K⊂S³, u(K) is the minimum crossing-change count needed to unknot it, over all diagrams. Write # for connected sum; 10₆ denotes the Rolfsen-table knot.

**Source.** Mark Brittenham, Susan Hermiller. *Unknotting number and connected sums: The knots 4_1 and 5_1*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.18757v1) Location: Conjecture 5.1, p. 15.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2926, 2927, checked 7 October 2026: Gebel–Prangley (August 2026) explicitly retains both; current computational errata distinguish minimal-diagram results from ordinary unknotting numbers.
