## Q2930. Metric properness of a quotient wreath product

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 8) · **Collection** 30

Does W admit an action by automorphisms on a median graph such that {g∈W:d(v,gv)≤R} is finite for every R≥0 and a vertex v?

**Context.** Origin for question 2930: Genevois’s explicit Baumslag–Solitar quotient case of the permutational-wreath question. Setup for question 2930: Let F₂=⟨a,t⟩ map onto BS(1,2)=⟨a,t | tat⁻¹=a²⟩ by the named generators, and let N be its kernel. Form W=(⊕\_{F₂/N}Z/2Z)⋊F₂ using left translation on the index set. A median graph has a unique common vertex of the three pairwise geodesic intervals for every triple.

**Source.** Anthony Genevois. *Problem 8 in Median Geometry and Applications, Oberwolfach Report 8/2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/53603) Location: Problem 8(i), printed p. 531.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2930, checked 7 October 2026: The February 2026 problem session leaves this case open. July’s Grigorchuk obstruction concerns a different group; no resolution found.

## Q3023. Low Khovanov groups of L-space knots

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.7) · **Collection** 31

For every L-space knot K, is Kh^{2,\*}(K) torsion-free, with Kh^{0,2g(K)±1}(K)=Z and all other Kh^{i,j}(K)=0 for i≤1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3023, 3024, 3025: Khovanov targets: Kegel–Manikandan–Mousseau–Silvero. The unknotting equality is an earlier Murasugi–Przytycki question, restated by Stoimenow. Setup for questions 3023, 3024, 3025: Knots lie in S³. Positive means admitting an all-positive diagram. An L-space knot admits positive surgery Y with rank HF̂(Y;F₂)=|H₁(Y;Z)|. Kh^{i,j} is integral unreduced Khovanov homology; g is Seifert genus.

**Source.** Naageswaran Manikandan. *Obstructions to positivity using Khovanov type theories*. 2025. Advisor(s): Chris Wendl, co-supervisor Marc Kegel. [primary source](https://edoc.hu-berlin.de/bitstreams/7f54a8cf-22ab-43a0-a603-779e7b64de9c/download) Location: Khovanov paper v2, Conjecture 1.7, p. 4.

## Q3025. Unknotting genus of positive fibered knots

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.0.1) · **Collection** 31

For every positive fibered knot K, is its unknotting number u(K) equal to g(K)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3023, 3024, 3025: Khovanov targets: Kegel–Manikandan–Mousseau–Silvero. The unknotting equality is an earlier Murasugi–Przytycki question, restated by Stoimenow. Setup for questions 3023, 3024, 3025: Knots lie in S³. Positive means admitting an all-positive diagram. An L-space knot admits positive surgery Y with rank HF̂(Y;F₂)=|H₁(Y;Z)|. Kh^{i,j} is integral unreduced Khovanov homology; g is Seifert genus.

**Source.** Naageswaran Manikandan. *Obstructions to positivity using Khovanov type theories*. 2025. Advisor(s): Chris Wendl, co-supervisor Marc Kegel. [primary source](https://edoc.hu-berlin.de/bitstreams/7f54a8cf-22ab-43a0-a603-779e7b64de9c/download) Location: Thesis Conjecture 2.0.1, p. 46; joint unknotting paper Conjecture 1.1.

## Q3028. Tight pairs and Reebless foliations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 31

For M≠S¹×S², does M carry a cooriented Reebless C⁰ foliation exactly when it carries a tight positive contact pair?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3028, 3029, 3030: Thomas Massoni. Symplectic and contact aspects of foliations and Anosov flows in dimension three. PhD, Princeton University, 2024. Advisors: John Pardon. Origin for questions 3028, 3029, 3030: Paper-origin questions, repeated in the 2026 survey. Setup for questions 3028, 3029, 3030: M is a closed connected oriented smooth 3-manifold. A contact pair comprises cooriented negative/positive contact structures ξ₋,ξ₊; positive means a common positively transverse vector field, tight means both structures tight. It is algebraically tight if ⟨c(ξ₋),c(ξ₊)⟩=1 in the Poincaré-duality pairing of reduced monopole Floer homology over F=F₂. Contact classes mean their reduced images. C⁰ foliations have C¹ leaves and continuous tangent planes.

**Source.** Thomas Massoni. *Taut foliations and contact pairs in dimension three*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.15635v2) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 14.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3028, 3029, 3030, checked 8 October 2026: August revision retains all three; no resolution located.

## Q3033. Infinite families in the missing genera

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.13) · **Collection** 31

Do infinitely many pairwise inequivalent Lefschetz fibrations with diffeomorphic total spaces exist in a fixed odd fiber genus? In fiber genus 2?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3033, 3034: Seraphina Eun Bi Lee. Mapping class groups of del Pezzo surfaces and Lefschetz fibrations. PhD, University of Chicago, 2025. Advisors: Benson Farb. Origin for questions 3033, 3034: Joint-paper questions, subsequent to Lee’s dissertation. Setup for questions 3033, 3034: A Lefschetz fibration π:X⁴→S² is a smooth surjection with closed connected oriented smooth total space and fiber, positive complex-Morse critical points with distinct critical values, and no fiber containing a (−1)-sphere. Equivalence means commuting diffeomorphisms of total spaces and bases. Hyperelliptic means monodromy centralizes a hyperelliptic involution of the fiber.

**Source.** Seraphina Eun Bi Lee and Carlos A. Serván. *Infinitely many Lefschetz pencils on ruled surfaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.10051v1) Location: Question 1.13, p. 5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3033, 3034, checked 8 October 2026: Latest version retains both; October 2026 handlebody-monodromy results impose extra hypotheses and do not resolve them.

## Q3035. Controlled homotopies near hypertaut foliations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6) · **Collection** 31

For every hypertaut C¹ foliation F and neighborhood U of TF, is there V⊂U containing TF such that any two positive (respectively negative) contact structures in V are homotopic through contact structures inside U?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3035, 3036: see question 3028. Origin for questions 3035, 3036: Controlled approximation: Bowden–Massoni. Flow rigidity: Hozoori’s earlier question. Setup for questions 3035, 3036: M is a closed connected oriented smooth 3-manifold; foliations and contact structures are cooriented. Hypertaut means a smooth exact 2-form is positive on the foliation’s tangent planes. All plane-field neighborhoods use the C⁰ topology. A projectively Anosov flow is smooth and nonsingular with invariant line splitting Eˢ⊕Eᵘ of TM/⟨X⟩ and ||dφ\_t|Eˢ||/||dφ\_t|Eᵘ||≤Ce^{−at}, t≥0, for C,a>0. Oriented Anosov flows have orientable stable/unstable bundles.

**Source.** Jonathan Bowden and Thomas Massoni. *Topological invariance of Liouville structures for taut foliations and Anosov flows*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.15325v2) Location: Question 6, p. 12.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3035, 3036, checked 8 October 2026: July 10 revision retains the questions; no subsequent resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15325) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/thomasmassoni/home) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11784-025-01192-5)

<a id="q3037"></a>

## Q3037. Tied Jones beyond Kauffman

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 31

Do two oriented links in S³ have identical two-variable Kauffman polynomials but distinct J_t when each component receives its own color?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3037: O’Bryan Alexander Cárdenas Andaur. PhD, University of Sevilla, 2026. Advisors: Marithania Silvero Casanova. Origin for question 3037: Cárdenas-Andaur’s 2024 paper question, retained in the thesis. Setup for question 3037: A tied link partitions its components into color classes. The AJ-bracket P(D)∈Z[A^{±1},c] is invariant under Reidemeister II–III, equals 1 on the unknot, and multiplies by c upon adding a new-color split circle, or by −A²−A⁻² for an existing-color circle. Monochromatic crossings obey P(crossing)=A P(A-smoothing)+A⁻¹P(B-smoothing). For a dichromatic crossing, P(crossing)+P(switched)=(A+A⁻¹)(P(A-smoothing)+P(B-smoothing)), merging the two color classes globally in either smoothing. For oriented D, J_t(L)=(-A)^{-3w(D)}P(D).

**Source.** O’Bryan Cárdenas Andaur. *Tied links and polynomial invariants*. 2026. Advisor(s): Marithania Silvero; tutor Juan González-Meneses. [primary source](https://idus.us.es/bitstreams/7e6030b3-c30c-4b1d-900e-caac493b2f56/download) Location: §5.3, p. 141; §5.4, p. 142; definitions pp. 39–43.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3037, checked 8 October 2026: September 2026 institutional deposit retains the question; earlier example calculations are corrected.

**Further links.** [1](https://idus.us.es/items/b6b0f574-859f-434f-ba84-e2ddc81e5ac5) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02075) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06259v1) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06259v2)

## Q3121. Perfectness of Euclidean scissors automorphism groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture E) · **Collection** 32

For every n≥2 and every n-dimensional Euclidean polytope P, is Aut(P) perfect?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3121: Ezekiel Lemann. Some results on scissors congruence and K-theory. PhD, 2025, Binghamton University. Advisors: Cary Malkiewich. Origin for question 3121: Zakharevich’s K₁-vanishing conjecture, reformulated in joint paper. Setup for question 3121: A Euclidean polytope is a finite union of full-dimensional simplices, possibly nonconvex or disconnected. For nonempty P, Aut(P) consists of its finite piecewise-isometric rearrangements, identifying maps agreeing away from lower-dimensional pieces; all Euclidean isometries are allowed.

**Source.** Alexander Kupers, Ezekiel Lemann, Cary Malkiewich, Jeremy Miller and Robin Janik Sroka. *Scissors automorphism groups I: Homological stability and K-theory*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.08081v3) Location: Conjecture E, p. 5; Definition 1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3121, checked 8 October 2026: September 23 revision retains the general conjecture. A September 17 seminar announces the n=2 case; higher dimensions are not covered by that announcement.

## Q3123. Homotopy representations for compact Lie groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 32

Is the canonical map V(G)→V′(G) an isomorphism for every compact Lie group G?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3123: Erik Knutsen. Homotopy Representations in the Equivariant Stable Homotopy Category. PhD, 2024, University of Colorado Boulder. Advisors: Agnès Beaudry. Origin for question 3123: Fausk–Lewis–May Conjecture 4.5, restated and partially solved by Knutsen. Setup for question 3123: For a compact Lie group G, a generalized homotopy representation is a finite-dimensional based G-CW complex of finite orbit type with Xᴴ≃S^{n(H)}, n(H)≥0, for each closed H≤G. It is a homotopy representation if dim Xᴴ=n(H). Let V′(G),V(G) be the respective Grothendieck groups of G-homotopy classes under smash product.

**Source.** Erik Knutsen. *Homotopy Representations and the Picard Group of the Equivariant Stable Homotopy Category*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.11915v1) Location: Conjecture 1.3, p. 2; Definition 2.6, pp. 4–5.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3123, checked 8 October 2026: The source proves the map is an isomorphism for finite G and whenever all projective Z[G/G⁰]-modules are stably free; general compact Lie groups remain the target.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.colorado.edu/math/graduate-program/cu-boulder-mathematics-theses)

## Q3219. Classical-link Thompson index

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.5) · **Collection** 33

For every classical link type L in S³, is ind_V(L)=ind_F(L)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3219, 3220: Louisa Liles. Quantum invariants and their connections with Thompson’s groups F and T. PhD, 2025, University of Virginia. Advisors: Slava Krushkal. Origin for questions 3219, 3220: Joint paper-origin questions. Setup for questions 3219, 3220: V is Thompson’s group of reduced binary tree-pairs with leaf permutation. L_V is the paper’s canonical unoriented virtual-link map (§3.1): take the boundary after joining the two tree-associated checkerboard surfaces according to that permutation. V⃗ comprises triples whose resulting checkerboard surface is orientable (Definition 4.1.3). For a classical link L, ind_F(L) and ind_V(L) are the minimum tree-leaf counts realizing L via Jones’s F-map and L_V, respectively.

**Source.** Micah Chrisman, Louisa Liles and Melody Molander. *Thompson’s Group V and Virtual Link Theory*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.28406v1) Location: Question 6.5, p. 34.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3219, 3220, checked 8 October 2026: The July 2026 preprint explicitly retains these questions.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 34

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.3) · **Collection** 34

For every n and 2&lt;r&lt;n, is VR(Z^n;r) homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3325, 3326: Raju Kumar Gupta. A characterization of normal 3-pseudomanifolds with a few singularities. Defense December11,2024; degree awarded August2,2025, Indian Institute of Technology Delhi. Advisors: Biplab Basak. Origin for questions 3325, 3326: Joint-paper questions. Setup for questions 3325, 3326: Equip Z^n and {0,1}^n with the ℓ¹ metric. VR(X;r) contains every finite subset of X of diameter≤r. Homology below is reduced integral homology.

**Source.** Raju Kumar Gupta, Sourav Sarkar and Samir Shukla. *On the Vietoris-Rips Complexes of Integer Lattices*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.04238v1) Location: Question 5.3, p. 31.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3325, 3326, checked 8 October 2026: Current author publication lists still mark the paper submitted; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.sav.sk/uploads/users_cv/20260112085741_317_CV.pdf)

## Q3331. Bridge number versus trivializing number

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

Must 2br(K)−2≤tr(K)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3331: Michał Jabłonowski. Niezmienniki lokalnych ruchów powierzchni w kowymiarze 2 [Invariants for Local Codimension-Two Surface Moves]. PhD, 2013, University of Gdańsk. Advisors: Witold Rosicki. Origin for question 3331: Paper-origin candidate inequalities. Setup for question 3331: Let K be a nontrivial smooth knot in S³, with bridge number br(K). For a projection P, tr(P) is the least number of crossings at which one can prescribe over/under information so that every resolution of the others is the unknot. Set tr(K)=min{tr(P): P underlies a diagram of K}.

**Source.** Michał Jabłonowski. *Integer Knot Invariants: Inequalities, Computations, and Open Problems*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22652v5) Location: §5, candidate (c2), p. 20; Appendix A, pp. 27–28.

## Q3457. Completing a grid using blank stabilizations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.13) · **Collection** 35

Can every grid diagram of a link be transformed into a triple grid by finitely many blank stabilizations?

**Context.** Origin: Thesis conjecture; stronger than the same thesis’s single-link representability question. Setup: A toroidal n×n grid diagram has two dots in every occupied row and column. A blank stabilization inserts an empty row and an empty column, preserving the orders of existing rows and columns and introducing no dots. A triple grid additionally has either zero or two dots on every toroidal diagonal c−r mod n.

**Source.** Sarah Blackwell. *Combinatorial and Group Theoretic Approaches to Trisected Surfaces in 4-Manifolds*. University of Georgia, 2022. Advisor(s): David Gay. [primary source](https://openscholar.uga.edu/record/3870/files/blackwell_sarah_e_202208_phd.pdf) Location: Conjecture 3.13, p. 38; Definition 3.12, p. 37; diagonal convention p. 36.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Later slides claim single-link representability using additional vertices. The stronger blank-stabilization assertion remains unresolved in inspected sources (2026-10-09).

<a id="q3459"></a>

## Q3459. Uncountable homological torsion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question F) · **Collection** 35

Do some infinite-type S and i≥1 have an uncountable torsion subgroup in H_i(Map(S)) or H_i(PMap(S))?

**Context.** Origin: Habilitation questions, related to earlier joint work with Xiaolei Wu. Setup: Surfaces are connected, orientable second-countable, and have compact boundary. Map(S) is the discrete group of orientation-preserving homeomorphisms modulo isotopy, fixing boundary pointwise; PMap(S) fixes each end. Homology and cohomology have integral coefficients. Infinite type means π₁(S) is infinitely generated; c=2^{ℵ₀}.

**Source.** Martin Palmer-Anghel. *Moduli spaces and their fundamental groups: homology, representations and lower central series*. 2024. [primary source](https://mdp.ac/habilitation/habilitation-thesis.pdf) Location: Question F.2.2, p. 182.

**Literature check.** Status (9 October 2026): Later compact-support and acyclic-group results do not settle these targets; no resolution was located.

## Q3463. What is tw(S¹×S¹×S¹), known to lie in {2,3,4}?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 35

**Context.** Doctoral context: Kristóf Huszár, ISTA PhD 2020, advisors Uli Wagner and Jonathan Spreer; Jeff Erickson, Berkeley PhD 1996, advisor Raimund Seidel. Origin: Report questions by Burton–Huszár–Spreer–Yuditsky and Erickson. Setup: A generalized triangulation T pairs tetrahedral faces, allowing unpaired boundary faces; Γ(T) has tetrahedra as vertices and gluings as edges. Treewidth is minimum largest-bag-size minus one over tree-indexed bag covers, with each edge in a bag and each vertex’s bags forming a subtree. Set tw(M)=min_T tw(Γ(T)).

**Source.** Dagstuhl Seminar 25201 participants. *Dagstuhl Seminar 25201 report*. 2025. [primary source](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/storage/04dagstuhl-reports/volume15/issue05/25201/DagRep.15.5.64/DagRep.15.5.64.pdf) Location: §5.7.1 Question 2, pp. 81–82.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Unresolved in inspected sources through 2026-10-09.

## Q3518. Classical transverse knots inside virtual transverse knots

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 36

Is the natural map from transverse knots to VTK injective?

**Context.** Advisors: Peter Ozsváth. Source type: PhD dissertation Origin: Thesis conjectures, repeated in the author’s subsequent paper. Setup: Let VB_n be the virtual braid group, generated by σ\_i^{±1},v_i with v_i²=1, distant commutation, and s_i t_{i+1}s_i=s_{i+1}t_i s_{i+1} for (s,t)=(σ,σ),(v,σ),(v,v). Let VTK be one-component closures modulo conjugation and positive stabilization β↦σ\_nβ. Ordinary braids with these equivalences represent transverse knots in standard contact three-space. A delta move passes a strand through a clasp. Degree-n delta finite type means the alternating sum over every (n+1)-cube of disjoint delta moves vanishes.

**Source.** Cole Wilkening Hugelmeyer. *Generalized State-Sum Invariants of Knots*. Princeton University, 2022. Advisor(s): Peter Ozsváth. [primary source](https://theses-dissertations.princeton.edu/items/122b395e-8fb4-443e-88ba-48b17f65da53) Location: Thesis Conjecture 2, p.36; paper Conjecture 2, p.21.

**Literature check.** Status: These conjectures persist in arXiv:2307.07661v1 (2023); no later resolution located, checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.07661)

<a id="q3521"></a>

## Q3521. Crossing-number bound on virtual periods

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 7.1) · **Collection** 36

If a nontrivial virtual knot K admits a q-periodic diagram, must q≤cr(K)?

**Context.** Advisors: Hans U. Boden, Andrew J. Nicas. Source type: PhD dissertation Origin: The student’s conjecture; related joint 2018 paper develops invariant-based restrictions on periods. Setup: A virtual knot is a planar knot diagram with classical and virtual crossings, modulo generalized Reidemeister moves. Its real crossing number cr(K) minimizes classical crossings over all virtual diagrams. A q-periodic diagram avoids the origin and is invariant under rotation through 2π/q, preserving crossing data.

**Source.** Lindsay White. *Alexander Invariants of Periodic Virtual Knots*. McMaster University, 2016. Advisor(s): Hans U. Boden, Andrew J. Nicas. [primary source](https://math.mcmaster.ca/~boden/students/White-PhD.pdf) Location: Conjecture 7.1, printed p.75 / PDF p.83.

**Literature check.** Status: No proof or counterexample located by 2026-10-09. Later work identifies virtual periods of classical knots with classical periods, leaving this general virtual-knot bound distinct.


<a id="q3522"></a>

## Q3522. Crossing counts of welded alternating diagrams

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.2) · **Collection** 36

Must any two reduced alternating diagrams representing the same non-split welded link have the same number of classical crossings?

**Context.** Degree: PhD (Mathematics), 2018 Advisors: Hans U. Boden. Source type: Dissertation-led paper follow-up Origin: Boden–Karimi, Classical results for alternating virtual links, NYJM 28 (2022), 1372–1398. The welded Tait program also appears in Boden’s 2019 BIRS problems. Setup: Welded equivalence adds the overcrossings-commute move to virtual Reidemeister moves. Alternation concerns only classical crossings. A diagram is split when its Gauss diagram is disconnected; a self-crossing is nugatory when its oriented smoothing is split. Reduced means having no nugatory crossings.

**Source.** Hans U. Boden and Homayun Karimi. *Classical results for alternating virtual links*. McMaster University, 2022. [primary source](https://nyjm.albany.edu/j/2022/28-58p.pdf) Location: Problem 7.2, p.1393; formulation immediately before Problem 7.1, p.1392.

**Literature check.** Status: Published 2022 questions; no resolution found by 2026-10-09. The same paper disproves writhe invariance, a different Tait assertion.

**Further links.** [1](https://math.mcmaster.ca/~boden/students/Karimi-PhD.pdf)

## Q3524. Motion planning for indistinguishable disks

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

Determine TC_r(U(n,w)) for integers n>w≥3 and r≥2.

**Context.** Degree: PhD, Spring 2023 Advisors: Jennifer Wilson. Source type: Dissertation-led paper follow-up Origin: The sequential (distributional) topological complexity of the ordered configuration space of disks in a strip, BLMS 58 (2026), e70421. Setup: For r≥2, TC_r(X) is the least k for which X^r has a (k+1)-set open cover carrying continuous sections of γ↦(γ(0),γ(1/(r−1)),…,γ(1)); use ∞ when disconnected. Let U(n,w) be configurations of n indistinguishable open unit-diameter disks in ℝ×[0,w], with disjoint interiors. Let F(n;p,q) similarly describe labeled open axis-parallel unit squares in [0,p]×[0,q].

**Source.** Nicholas Graham Wawrykow. *The sequential (distributional) topological complexity of the ordered configuration space of disks in a strip*. University of Michigan, 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1112/blms.70421) Location: Section 4, question (1).

**Literature check.** Status: Questions remain in the July 2026 published version; checked 2026-10-09. Width-two unordered disks are already solved.

**Further links.** [1](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1112/blms.70421) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19943)

## Q3624. Prime-part equipartitions on codimension-variable sections

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 37

Given N≥p−1, a convex body K⊂R^N with 0∈int(K), and N−1 continuous real functions on full-dimensional compact convex bodies within linear (p−1)-planes, must some linear (p−1)-plane V admit a partition of K∩V into p convex pieces of equal volume and equal values of every function?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. Origin: Student’s Open Questions 1 and 6. Setup: Use F_p coefficients, p an odd prime. For a real vector bundle E→B over a CW-complex, let F_p(E)={(v_1,…,v_p):v_i are distinct and lie in one fibre}; S_p permutes coordinates. Cohomology is a module over H\*(B)⊗H\*(BS_p) through projection and the classifying map of the covering F_p(E)→F_p(E)/S_p. Convex-body spaces carry the fibrewise Hausdorff topology.

**Source.** Tatiana Levinson. *Convex partitions of vector bundles and fibrewise configuration spaces*. Freie Universität Berlin, 2023. Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1303907593/34) Location: §1.3, Open Question 1, p.17; formal setup §2.1.

**Literature check.** Status: No subsequent general solution or newer publication located in bounded searches through 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://refubium.fu-berlin.de/handle/fub188/40883?locale-attribute=en) · [2](https://www.mi.fu-berlin.de/fb/dates/disputationen/Levinson_-Tatiana---MI---Aushang.pdf)


<a id="q3625"></a>

## Q3625. Even-rank unordered fibrewise configuration cohomology

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6) · **Collection** 37

For arbitrary even-rank E, determine H\*(F_p(E)/S_p) as an H\*(B)⊗H\*(BS_p)-module.

**Context.** Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. Origin: Student’s Open Questions 1 and 6. Setup: Use F_p coefficients, p an odd prime. For a real vector bundle E→B over a CW-complex, let F_p(E)={(v_1,…,v_p):v_i are distinct and lie in one fibre}; S_p permutes coordinates. Cohomology is a module over H\*(B)⊗H\*(BS_p) through projection and the classifying map of the covering F_p(E)→F_p(E)/S_p. Convex-body spaces carry the fibrewise Hausdorff topology.

**Source.** Tatiana Levinson. *Convex partitions of vector bundles and fibrewise configuration spaces*. Freie Universität Berlin, 2023. Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1303907593/34) Location: §1.3, Open Question 6, p.18.

**Literature check.** Status: No subsequent general solution or newer publication located in bounded searches through 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://refubium.fu-berlin.de/handle/fub188/40883?locale-attribute=en) · [2](https://www.mi.fu-berlin.de/fb/dates/disputationen/Levinson_-Tatiana---MI---Aushang.pdf)


<a id="q3626"></a>

## Q3626. Alexander-refinement support exchange

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.6) · **Collection** 37

For α,β∈J and α\_i>β\_i, must some j with α\_j<β\_j satisfy α−e_i+e_j,β+e_i−e_j∈J, where e_i are standard basis vectors?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Ilya Kofman. Paper: An Alexander Polynomial Refinement for Alternating Links, with Trapezoidal Properties Origin: Boninger’s Conjectures 1.6–1.7; definitions §§3–4. Setup: For a nonsplit oriented alternating link diagram, shade north/south sectors when the overstrand runs southwest–northeast. Direct Tait edges between shaded regions with the overstrand; choose a root vertex incident to the outer face, with basepoint there. For spanning tree T, count positive edges in T pointing away/toward root by a,b, and negative edges outside T agreeing/opposing clockwise fundamental-cycle orientation by c,d. Put P=Σ\_T x^(−a)y^(−b)z^cw^d, with coefficients c_α and support J.

**Source.** Joe Boninger. *An Alexander Polynomial Refinement for Alternating Links, with Trapezoidal Properties*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.28484v1) Location: Conjecture 1.6, p.4.

**Literature check.** Status: August 2026 v1 is current; no resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/gc_etds/4803/) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/joeboninger)

<a id="q3628"></a>

## Q3628. Positive ribbon minimality

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 37

If K is positive and J≤K, must J be isotopic to K?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Ilya Kofman. Paper: Positive knots and ribbon concordance, Pacific Journal of Mathematics 335 (2025), 81–95 Origin: Boninger–Greene and Tagami’s minimality conjecture; Stoimenow’s concordance conjecture. Setup: Knots lie in S³. Positive means admitting an all-positive crossing diagram. Smooth concordance is an embedded annulus in S³×[0,1]. Write J≤K if such an annulus joins J×{0} to K×{1} with Morse height having no index-two critical points.

**Source.** Joe Boninger. *Positive knots and ribbon concordance*. 2025. [primary source](https://msp.org/pjm/2025/335-1/pjm-v335-n1-p04-p.pdf) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p.82.

**Literature check.** Status: Final 2025 text; no general resolution found through 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/gc_etds/4803/) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/joeboninger/research)

## Q3630. Sharp dimensions for regular maps

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 37

Determine N(d,k,F) for all d,k and both fields.

**Context.** Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. Origin: Borsuk’s older problem; the student proves new lower bounds. Setup: For integers d,k≥1 and F∈{R,C}, a continuous map f:R^d→F^N is k-regular if the images of every k distinct points are linearly independent over F. Define N(d,k,F) as the least such target dimension N.

**Source.** Evgeniya Lagoda. *k-regular maps and cohomology theory of configuration spaces*. Freie Universität Berlin, 2025. Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1370897782/34) Location: Summary p.i; Definition 1.2, p.49; N(d,k,F), p.50.

**Literature check.** Status: The 2025 thesis leaves general sharp values undetermined; no general resolution located through 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://refubium.fu-berlin.de/handle/fub188/47773?show=full) · [2](https://elagoda.github.io/)

## Q3632. Dihedral persistence of regular polygons

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 37

For a regular k-gon X_k⊂S¹, k≥3, determine H_{D_{2k}}\*(VR(X_k;r)) under its order-2k dihedral symmetry, for all r≥0.

**Context.** Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. Paper: Persistent equivariant cohomology — Adams, Lagoda, Moy, Sadovek, De Saha Origin: Joint-paper Questions 3 and 8. Setup: Write H_G\*(Y)=H\*(EG×\_G Y;Z), as graded rings, including every inclusion-induced persistence map. VR(X;r) has finite subsets of diameter≤r as simplices. S¹ is the unit circle with geodesic distance; Q_n={0,1}^n has Hamming distance.

**Source.** Adams, Lagoda, Moy, Sadovek and De Saha. *Persistent equivariant cohomology*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.17331v1) Location: §8, Question 3, p.25.

**Literature check.** Status: Current v1; no general solutions located.

<a id="q3634"></a>

## Q3634. Toroidal colored-Jones volume conjecture

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2) · **Collection** 37

If (T²×[0,1])\L is hyperbolic, must lim_{n→∞}(2π/n)log|J_n^T(L;e^{2πi/n})| equal its hyperbolic volume?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Ilya Kofman. Paper: A quantum invariant of links in T²×I with volume conjecture behavior, AGT 23, 1891–1934 Origin: Boninger’s 2020 preprint conjecture, incorporated into his 2022 thesis; retained in the 2023 final. Setup: For an oriented link L⊂T²×[0,1], let J_n^T(L;q) be Boninger’s toroidal colored Jones polynomial: color every strand by the n-dimensional U_q(sl₂)-module; cut a diagram along a fundamental square, evaluate the §4.1-normalized R-matrix/cup/cap operators, and take the ordinary trace over matching cut strands. Multiply by q^{−(n²−1)w(D)/4}, where w(D) is diagram writhe. There is no division by the quantum dimension [n]=(q^{n/2}−q^{−n/2})/(q^{1/2}−q^{−1/2}).

**Source.** Joe Boninger. *A quantum invariant of links in T²×I with volume conjecture behavior*. 2023. [primary source](https://msp.org/agt/2023/23-4/agt-v23-n4-p12-p.pdf) Location: Conjecture 1.2, p.1892; definitions §§3–4, especially 4.4, p.1910.

**Literature check.** Status: Verified for the 2×2 square weave; no general resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/gc_etds/4803/) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/joeboninger/research)


<a id="q3725"></a>

## Q3725. Minimal diagrams detect two-adjacency

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 7.1) · **Collection** 38

Does every minimal diagram of every nontrivial alternating 2-adjacent knot contain a 2-adjacency set?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Tye Lidman. Paper: Constructing and Cataloging 2-Adjacent Knots, with John Carney Origin: Thesis §5.7; later paper §7. Setup: A 2-adjacency set in a knot diagram consists of two distinct crossings such that switching either crossing alone, or both, produces the unknot. A knot is 2-adjacent if some diagram has such a set. A minimal diagram realizes its crossing number. Alternating means admitting a diagram alternating over/under along the knot.

**Source.** John Carney and Everett August Meike. *Constructing and Cataloging 2-Adjacent Knots*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.00291v1) Location: Paper Conjecture 7.1, p.19; thesis Conjecture 5.7.1, p.54.

**Literature check.** Status: September 2025 v1 current; no resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://repository.lib.ncsu.edu/items/9e1792b1-5564-482a-990a-722dbe576014) · [2](https://sites.google.com/ncsu.edu/tlid/home) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00291)

## Q3727. Rational anisotropy of positive knots

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.8) · **Collection** 38

Is every positive knot Q-anisotropic?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Ilya Kofman. Paper: Positive knots and ribbon concordance, Pacific Journal of Mathematics 335 (2025), 81–95 Origin: Boninger’s Conjecture 1.8. Setup: Knots lie in S³. Positive means admitting an all-positive crossing diagram. For the infinite cyclic cover X of a knot exterior, Q-anisotropic means H¹(X;Q) has no nonzero deck-invariant subspace on which the cup pairing H¹(X;Q)×H¹(X,∂X;Q)→H²(X,∂X;Q)≅Q vanishes; identify absolute and relative H¹ naturally.

**Source.** Joe Boninger. *Positive knots and ribbon concordance*. 2025. [primary source](https://msp.org/pjm/2025/335-1/pjm-v335-n1-p04-p.pdf) Location: Conjecture 1.8, p.84; definition §5, p.91.

**Further links.** [1](https://academicworks.cuny.edu/gc_etds/4803/) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/joeboninger/research)


<a id="q3728"></a>

## Q3728. Graphs with cocollapsible cotrees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

Find a structural graph-theoretic characterization of those G for which every 1-cotree of K is cocollapsible.

**Context.** Advisor(s): Herbert Edelsbrunner. Origin: Thesis discussion question. Setup: For a finite connected simple graph G let K be its clique complex. Over F₂, call an edge set S a 1-cotree if its rows of the triangle-to-edge boundary matrix are linearly independent. Start L with all vertices and edges outside S. A cocollapse adds to L a missing edge e and triangle τ whose other two edges already belong to L. S is cocollapsible if successive such moves add every edge of S.

**Source.** Ondřej Draganov. *Structures and computations in topological data analysis*. Institute of Science and Technology Austria, 2025. Advisor(s): Herbert Edelsbrunner. [primary source](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/download/18979/19000/Thesis.pdf) Location: §5.5 Discussion, p.126; definitions pp.116,121,123.

**Literature check.** Status: Open in 2025 thesis; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/record/18979)


<a id="q3729"></a>

## Q3729. Universal linear triangulated filling bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

Is there an absolute constant C such that every (M,T) with n triangles admits a filling with at most Cn tetrahedra, independently of the genus?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Jean-François Lafont. Paper: Filling triangulated surfaces, with Lafont and Barry Minemyer Origin: Paper’s genus-independent filling question; thesis supplies earlier systolic methods. Setup: A filling of a triangulated closed oriented surface (M,T) is a compact triangulated 3-manifold (W,U) with ∂W=M and boundary triangulation exactly T, without subdividing T. The filling manifold may depend on T.

**Source.** Ryan Kowalick, Jean-François Lafont and Barry Minemyer. *Filling triangulated surfaces*. 2019. [primary source](https://par.nsf.gov/servlets/purl/10121108) Location: §1, paragraph following Theorem 2, p.1.

**Literature check.** Status: Reaffirmed in October 2019 author manuscript; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://etd.ohiolink.edu/acprod/odb_etd/r/etd/search/10?p10_accession_num=osu1384873457) · [2](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10711-018-00419-9)

## Q3730. Negative curvature of atoroidal surface bundles

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.2) · **Collection** 38

Must M admit a Riemannian metric whose sectional curvatures are everywhere strictly negative?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Jean-François Lafont. Paper: Lafont–Miller–Ruffoni, On signatures of the atoroidal bundles of Kent–Leininger (2024) Origin: Widely circulated question, restated in advisor’s earlier paper, not attributed to Cho. Setup: Let M be the total space of an oriented bundle of closed surfaces S_g→M→S_h, with g,h≥2. Assume M is atoroidal: π₁(M) contains no subgroup isomorphic to Z².

**Source.** Lafont, Miller and Ruffoni. *On signatures of the atoroidal bundles of Kent–Leininger*. 2024. [primary source](https://people.math.osu.edu/lafont.1/SignKL.pdf) Location: Question 1.2, second question, p.2; setup p.1.

**Literature check.** Status: Current author preprint; no later resolution located.
